# SpecKiwi MCP Stdio Interface SRS

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Document Type | scope_srs |
| Product | SpecKiwi |
| Product Version | 1.0.0 |
| Scope | MCP |
| Scope Name | MCP Stdio Interface |
| Version | 1.0.0 |
| Last Updated | 2026-05-13 |
| Rules | [SRS-MD Authoring Rules v2.5.0](../rule/SRS-MD-Rules-v2.5.0.md) |

## 1. Scope Overview

이 문서는 SpecKiwi stdio MCP server의 transport, tools, resources, security boundary, 오류 응답을 정의한다.

## 2. Scope Boundaries

### In Scope

- stdio MCP server
- read-only MCP tools
- mutation MCP tools
- project initialization MCP tool
- MCP resources
- MCP safety and filesystem boundary
- structured errors

### Out of Scope

- Streamable HTTP MCP server
- OAuth 또는 원격 인증
- 외부 웹 API 호출
- shell command execution
- MCP client 구현

## 3. Assumptions and Constraints

- MCP server는 `speckiwi mcp`로 실행된다.
- stdout은 JSON-RPC MCP message 전용이다.
- stderr는 log 전용이다.
- MCP server는 core service를 사용한다.

## 4. Requirements

### IR-MCP-001 — stdio MCP server 제공

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | interface |
| Target | v1.0.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | mcp, stdio, server |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | test, inspection |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi는 stdio transport를 사용하는 MCP server를 제공해야 한다.

#### Rationale

AI coding agent가 별도 CLI 사용법을 외우지 않고 표준 MCP tool 호출로 SRS를 사용할 수 있어야 한다.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `speckiwi mcp`가 MCP server process를 시작한다.
- [x] AC-2: server는 stdin에서 JSON-RPC 메시지를 읽고 stdout으로 응답한다.
- [x] AC-3: stdout에는 MCP 메시지 이외의 로그를 쓰지 않는다.
- [x] AC-4: 로그와 diagnostics는 stderr에 쓴다.
- [x] AC-5: MCP client가 `--root` 없이 server를 등록해도 server process의 현재 작업 디렉터리를 기준으로 project root를 결정한다.
- [x] AC-6: server startup 시 `docs/spec/00.index.md`가 없으면 MCP tool 또는 resource 요청을 처리하기 전에 SRS 기본 구조를 자동 생성한다.
- [x] AC-7: `.git`과 `docs/spec/00.index.md`를 모두 찾을 수 없는 빈 작업 디렉터리에서는 server process의 현재 작업 디렉터리를 project root로 삼아 자동 init한다.
- [x] AC-8: 명시적 `--root`가 존재하지 않거나 디렉터리가 아니면 자동 init 대상이 아니며 server startup은 오류를 반환한다.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/release/srs-traceability.test.ts; npm test; npm run test:integration; npm run release:acceptance; npm run perf:srs | all | SpecKiwi v1.0.0 implementation verification on 2026-05-08 |

#### Trace Links

| Type | Reference | Relation | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

#### Research / Analysis

- -

#### Implementation Notes

- MCP 공식 stdio transport 규칙을 따른다.
- Streamable HTTP transport는 v1.0.0 범위 밖이다.
- `--root`가 생략되면 `process.cwd()`에서 시작해 `.git` 또는 `docs/spec/00.index.md`가 있는 상위 디렉터리를 project root로 사용한다.
- root를 찾지 못하면 `process.cwd()` 자체를 root로 삼아 `initProject` bootstrap을 실행한다.
- 명시적 `--root`는 존재하는 directory만 허용한다. 존재하지 않는 명시 root는 오타 가능성이 있으므로 자동 생성하지 않는다.
- 자동 init은 server startup에서 수행해 read-only tool handler 자체가 정상 workspace에서는 파일을 변경하지 않게 한다.

#### Change Notes

| Date | Change | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-05-07 | 최초 작성 | SpecKiwi v1.0.0 제품 SRS baseline 정의 |
| 2026-05-09 | root 없는 MCP 등록의 cwd 기반 root 결정 요구 추가 | MCP client 설정에서 `--root`를 필수로 요구하지 않기 위함 |
| 2026-05-09 | MCP startup 자동 init 요구 추가 | index 누락 또는 빈 작업 디렉터리에서도 첫 MCP 호출이 실패하지 않게 함 |

### SEC-MCP-001 — MCP 서버 파일 접근 제한

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | security |
| Target | v1.0.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | mcp, security, filesystem |
| Risk | critical |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | test, inspection |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP server는 명시적 `--root` 또는 현재 작업 디렉터리 기준으로 결정된 저장소 루트 밖의 파일을 읽거나 쓰지 않아야 한다.

#### Rationale

MCP server는 agent가 호출하므로 파일 시스템 접근 범위를 명확히 제한해야 한다.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: relative path는 root 내부로 normalize된다.
- [x] AC-2: root 밖으로 나가는 path traversal은 거부한다.
- [x] AC-3: local file evidence 검증도 root 내부 기준으로 수행한다.
- [x] AC-4: MCP tool input으로 임의 shell command를 실행하지 않는다.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

#### Trace Links

| Type | Reference | Relation | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

- -

#### Implementation Notes

- shell execution은 v1.0.0 MCP scope에 포함하지 않는다.
- `--root`가 없는 등록에서는 현재 작업 디렉터리에서 resolve한 project root를 보안 경계로 사용한다.

#### Change Notes

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP server는 프로젝트 SRS 기본 구조를 생성하는 `init_project` tool을 제공해야 한다.

#### Rationale

AI coding agent는 SRS 구조가 아직 없는 저장소에서도 MCP를 통해 SpecKiwi 문서 구조를 안전하게 초기화할 수 있어야 한다.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `init_project` tool은 `target`, `scope`, `force`를 선택 입력으로 받는다.
- [x] AC-2: tool은 project root의 `docs/`, `docs/spec/`, `docs/rule/` 구조를 확인하고 없으면 생성한다.
- [x] AC-3: `docs/spec/00.index.md`가 없으면 요구사항이 없는 기본 SRS index 문서를 생성한다.
- [x] AC-4: `docs/spec/90.appendix.md`가 없으면 기본 appendix 문서를 생성한다.
- [x] AC-5: 번들 SRS-MD 규칙 문서(`BUNDLED_SRS_RULES_FILENAME` 가 이름을 정한다)가 없으면 canonical SRS-MD rules 문서를 생성한다.
- [x] AC-6: `init_project` tool은 대문자 `AGENTS.md`를 항상 생성 또는 갱신한다.
- [x] AC-7: `init_project` tool은 대문자 `CLAUDE.md`를 항상 생성 또는 갱신한다.
- [x] AC-8: agent 파일에 추가되는 managed block은 `AGENT_INSTRUCTION_HEADING_PREFIX` 가 정하는 versioned heading, `AGENT_INSTRUCTION_END_MARKER` 가 정하는 suffix marker, SRS source-of-truth 강제 문장, current work status workflow를 포함한다.
- [x] AC-9: 동일한 현재 버전 managed block이 이미 있으면 중복 삽입하지 않고, 다른 버전 또는 legacy unversioned block이 있으면 현재 버전 block으로 교체한다.
- [x] AC-10: 성공 시 created, skipped, updated path 목록과 diagnostics를 structured result로 반환한다.
- [x] AC-11: 실패 시 structured error를 반환하고 `force` 없이 기존 파일을 덮어쓰지 않는다.
- [x] AC-12: MCP server startup은 `docs/spec/00.index.md` 누락 시 `init_project`와 같은 bootstrap service를 자동으로 실행한다.

- tool은 mutation으로 분류한다.
- `agentFile` 또는 `agentFiles` 입력은 제공하지 않는다. agent 안내는 항상 `AGENTS.md`와 `CLAUDE.md` 양쪽에 적용한다.
- MCP server는 `docs/spec/00.index.md`가 아직 없어도 `.git` 또는 명시적 root로 project root를 결정할 수 있어야 한다.
- 초기화 전 SRS 조회/변경 tool이 index 누락으로 실패하지 않도록 server startup에서 자동 init을 먼저 수행한다.
- rules 문서의 canonical 위치는 `docs/rule/SRS-MD-Rules-v1.0.0.md`다.
- agent instruction의 현재 managed block version은 `v1.1`이다.

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP server는 Completed Work Log를 조회하는 `list_completed_work` tool과 완료 작업 요약을 추가하는 `add_completed_work` tool을 제공해야 한다.

#### Rationale

AI coding agent는 CLI를 직접 호출하지 않아도 MCP를 통해 현재 작업 상태와 완료 작업 요약을 안전하게 읽고 갱신할 수 있어야 한다.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `list_completed_work` input은 `target`, `scope`, `since`, `limit` 선택 field를 받는다.
- [x] AC-2: `list_completed_work`는 read-only tool annotation을 가진다.
- [x] AC-3: `list_completed_work` output은 `{ completedWork }` 객체이며 target filter는 targetless cross-target row를 포함한다.
- [x] AC-4: `add_completed_work` input은 `date`, `summary`, optional `target`, `scope`, `requirementIds`, `dryRun`을 받는다.
- [x] AC-5: `add_completed_work`는 mutation tool로 등록되며 read-only annotation을 갖지 않는다.
- [x] AC-6: `add_completed_work` dry-run과 write mode는 core mutation result를 반환한다.
- [x] AC-7: invalid date, invalid limit, pipe-containing input은 schema 또는 core mutation failure로 구조화되어 반환된다.

SpecKiwi MCP read tools must match CLI read payload semantics for validation diagnostics, target summaries, active-target summaries, and completed-work filtering.

#### Rationale

Coding agents should receive the same governance signals through MCP as a human receives through CLI, without bespoke interpretation per interface.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `validate_spec` returns a standardized `{ diagnostics, errors, warnings, summary }` style payload or an equivalent documented envelope.

- [x] AC-3: `get_active_target` returns the exact Active Target metadata value and expanded summary without fallback when the metadata value is empty.
- [x] AC-4: `list_completed_work` uses the same filters and latest ordering semantics as CLI completed-work reads.
- [x] AC-5: Contract tests compare overlapping CLI and MCP read payloads for shape and meaning.

### FR-MCP-014 — MCP resource payload policy

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP resources must use the v1.2.0 envelope-all payload policy across index, active-target, completed-work, requirement, target, and scope resources.

#### Rationale

Mixed raw and wrapped resource shapes force agents to special-case each resource and can hide diagnostics that are needed for safe automation.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The SRS appendix documents envelope-all as the selected v1.2.0 resource payload policy.
- [x] AC-2: Each resource payload includes `ok`, `value`, `diagnostics`, and `diagnosticsSummary` fields.
- [x] AC-3: The `value` field contains the resource-specific domain payload that raw-domain resources previously returned.
- [x] AC-4: Existing completed-work raw `{ completedWork }` resource compatibility impact and compatibility window are documented before payload shape changes.
- [x] AC-5: stdio MCP tests cover every resource shape under the selected policy.

### FR-MCP-015 — target registration and Active Target defaulting tools

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.1.0 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | mcp, tool, target, active-target, mutation |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test, inspection |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/spec/90.appendix.md](./90.appendix.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP server must let agents register a new target and make it the Active Target through a mutation tool, and `add_requirement` must default to the Active Target when no explicit target is provided.

#### Rationale

Agent-driven SRS authoring should not require raw Markdown edits to add a target row before writing requirements. The MCP contract must make the target source explicit so agents can explain whether a requirement used a user-provided target or the current Active Target.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `set_active_target` accepts an explicit create flag that registers a missing target before setting it active.
- [x] AC-2: Without the create flag, `set_active_target` continues to reject unknown targets without changing files.
- [x] AC-3: Target creation input supports target type and description, with deterministic defaults when omitted.
- [x] AC-4: `add_requirement.target` is optional in MCP input; when omitted, the tool uses the parsed Active Target.
- [x] AC-5: If `add_requirement.target` is omitted and Active Target is empty, the tool returns a structured error and does not mutate files.
- [x] AC-6: Mutation responses expose whether the target came from explicit input or Active Target defaulting.
- [x] AC-7: Tool schemas and stdio metadata document the new optional/defaulted target contract.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/mcp/mutation-tools-errors.test.ts; test/release/tool-signature-parity.test.ts | all | MCP target creation schema, Active Target defaulting, empty-target failure, and targetSource output. |

#### Trace Links

| Type | Reference | Relation | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Requirement | FR-NODE-014 | depends_on | MCP delegates target registration and default resolution to core services. |
| Requirement | IR-CLI-023 | related_to | CLI and MCP must share the same target semantics. |
| Requirement | FR-FLOW-011 | supports | Agent workflow uses MCP first for target-scoped SRS authoring. |

#### Research / Analysis

- Current MCP `set_active_target` rejects unknown targets, and `add_requirement` requires `target`.

#### Implementation Notes

- The create flag must be explicit to prevent accidental Target Map growth from typos.
- Existing clients that pass `target` explicitly must keep the same behavior.
- The MCP adapter should expose schema fields rather than relying on undocumented dynamic input.

| 2026-06-29 | Status -> implemented | v2.3.0 tool lifecycle, doctor, and signature parity implementation evidence attached |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP server must expose optional Completed Work Log report paths on add_completed_work and return report path lists through completed-work read surfaces.

#### Rationale

Coding agents primarily use MCP, so report path metadata must be available through the same interface that appends and reads Completed Work Log summaries.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: add_completed_work input schema accepts optional reportPaths as an array of non-empty strings and optional allowIncomplete as a boolean.
- [x] AC-2: MCP schema validation rejects blank reportPaths values and values that violate the FR-PARSE-016 report path grammar before invoking the shared mutation.
- [x] AC-3: MCP handler forwards reportPaths, allowIncomplete, and dryRun to the shared Completed Work Log core mutation.
- [x] AC-4: list_completed_work, summarize_target completedWork payloads, and completed-work resources include reportPaths arrays parsed from the index.
- [x] AC-5: Existing MCP clients that omit reportPaths continue to receive the previous fields unchanged plus an empty reportPaths array when entries are read.
- [x] AC-6: add_completed_work mutation results include reportPaths as a string array, including dry-run results.
- [x] AC-7: MCP tests verify stdio schema, mutation forwarding, dry-run behavior, resource/read parity for reportPaths, default-deny allowIncomplete behavior, and explicit allowIncomplete pass-through.

- The review also found an existing parity gap: MCP documentation mentions allowIncomplete forwarding, but the MCP schema and handler must explicitly expose it.

- Use reportPaths for the MCP field name to match the core and JSON output shape. Keep the field optional and avoid changing read-only annotations for list_completed_work.
- Keep additive read payload changes backwards compatible for clients that ignore unknown fields.

### REL-MCP-002 — MCP startup workspace user home 경계 격리

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | reliability |
| Target | v2.1.2 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | mcp, startup, workspace-isolation, regression-guard |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | inspection |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP server startup 시 SRS root 탐색은 사용자 홈 디렉터리 경계를 넘어서 상위로 ascend 하지 않으며, 홈 디렉터리 외부의 우발적 `docs/spec/00.index.md` 가 현재 cwd 의 SRS root 로 채택되어서는 안 된다.

#### Rationale

기존 startup resolver 는 `docs/spec/00.index.md` 또는 `.git` 을 찾을 때까지 부모 디렉터리를 무한 ascend 했다. 사용자 홈 (예: `C:\Users\<user>\docs\spec\00.index.md`) 에 stray SRS index 가 존재할 때, 빈 cwd (tempdir, 신규 프로젝트 폴더 등) 에서 MCP server 가 실행되면 stray index 가 채택되어 FR-MCP-011 AC-12 의 auto-init 이 실제 cwd 에 적용되지 않는 회귀가 발생한다.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: SRS root 탐색은 사용자 홈 디렉터리 (`os.homedir()`) 에서 중지하며 그 이상으로 ascend 하지 않는다.
- [x] AC-2: cwd 와 사용자 홈 사이에 `docs/spec/00.index.md` 가 존재하지 않으면 startup 은 현재 작업 디렉터리를 project root 로 도구적으로 선택한다.
- [x] AC-3: 사용자 홈 내부의 stray `docs/spec/00.index.md` 가 cwd 가 홈 외부에 있을 때 project root 로 치환되지 않는다.
- [x] AC-4: `--root` 옵션으로 명시적 root 를 넘긴 경우는 이 경계 규칙을 적용하지 않고 명시 경로를 그대로 사용한다.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | inspection | src/mcp/server.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4 | findSrsRootFrom() 는 `os.homedir()` 를 경계로 ascend 중단; 명시 root 는 explicitStartupRoot 로 분기 (commit 6f84882) |
| VE-2 | review | commit 6f84882 | all | fix(mcp): isolate MCP startup to the cwd SRS tree 으로 활성 cwd 근접 SRS 트리만 채택 |
| VE-3 | test | test/mcp/startup-cwd.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4 | - |

#### Trace Links

| Type | Reference | Relation | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Requirement | FR-MCP-011 | refines | FR-MCP-011 AC-12 startup auto-init 의 cwd 정확성 보강 |

#### Research / Analysis

- -

#### Implementation Notes

- `src/mcp/server.ts` 의 `findSrsRootFrom(start)` 가 `realpath(os.homedir())` 를 boundary 로 두고, cwd 부터 부모로 ascend 하면서 각 디렉터리에서 `docs/spec/00.index.md` 존재 여부를 검사한다. 홈 디렉터리에 도달하면 null 을 반환하고, `resolveMcpStartupRoot` 가 cwd 로 fallback 한다. 명시 `--root` 입력은 `explicitStartupRoot` 분기로 그대로 사용한다.

#### Change Notes

| Date | Change | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |

AI coding agent 가 SRS Stability 필드를 안전하게 갱신하려면 CLI 직접 호출 외에 MCP tool 경로가 필요하다. FR-MCP-004 (`update_status`) 와 동일 입출력 패턴을 stability 차원에 동형 노출.

### FR-MCP-018 — append_section_note mutation tool

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.2.2 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | mutation, documentation, governance |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP and CLI MUST expose an append_section_note mutation that appends a free-text note to a chosen REQ body section (Rationale, Research, or Implementation Notes), with an automatic timestamp prefix and runtime-validated section identifier. The tool MUST be req-scoped (single Requirement ID) and explicitly deny structured sections (Verification Evidence, Acceptance Criteria).

#### Rationale

Three-agent review (2026-05-16) converged on a single generalised tool over three per-section tools to reduce surface area. UX-agent flagged timestamp drift risk for cumulative appends; governance-agent flagged self-justification risk (agent could append 'verified by VE-3' text and have a future parser misread it as evidence). Solution: timestamp auto-prefix + explicit deny-list for structured sections + length/control-char limits identical to update_status reason policy.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: AC-1: input schema accepts {id: string, section: string, text: string, mode?: 'append'|'replace', dryRun?: boolean}; mode default = 'append'.
- [x] AC-2: AC-2: section identifier is validated at runtime against an allowlist sourced from rules (initial allowlist: rationale, research, implementation_notes); any other value yields USAGE error.
- [x] AC-3: AC-3: deny-list for 'verification_evidence' and 'acceptance_criteria' is enforced at the mutation core layer with MUTATION_DENIED, independent of allowlist contents.
- [x] AC-4: AC-4: text length limit = 500 UTF-16 code units; control characters other than TAB/LF/CR are rejected (reuse update_status reason validator).
- [x] AC-5: AC-5: append mode prepends a single-line ISO date prefix '[YYYY-MM-DD] ' to each note row; replace mode replaces the section body in a single optimistic-concurrency write, and an intervening external mutation since the snapshot read MUST be detected via SHA snapshot guard (no two-call dryRun protocol; stateless servers cannot enforce 'first call dryRun=true' reliably).
- [x] AC-6: AC-6: when the target section heading is missing, the mutation inserts the heading + the note as a single atomic patch (no two-step intermediate state).
- [x] AC-7: AC-7: an automated test asserts: (a) append succeeds for allowlisted sections, (b) deny-list sections are rejected with MUTATION_DENIED, (c) repeated append produces N timestamp-prefixed lines.
- [x] AC-8: AC-8: the tool is registered in src/mcp/tools/mutation-tools.ts only (not read-tools.ts) and is classified kind=req-scoped per FR-ARCH-005.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/core/mutation/append-section-note.test.ts; test/mcp/append-section-note-mcp.test.ts; test/cli/append-note-cli.test.ts; test/core/rules/section-allowlist.test.ts; npm test | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-7, AC-8 | v2.2.2 implementation verification on 2026-05-17 |

#### Trace Links

| Type | Reference | Relation | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

#### Research / Analysis

- Architecture-agent: prefer string section identifier with runtime allowlist over closed enum to avoid breaking-change every time a section is added or renamed. Allowed set sourced from SRS-MD-Rules. UX-agent: agents naturally call append after implementation step; tool description must declare 'call after implementation, not before' to prevent premature documentation. Governance-agent: deny-list MUST include 'verification_evidence' and 'acceptance_criteria' at code level (not docs only).

#### Implementation Notes

- Implement src/core/mutation/append-section-note.ts mirroring update-status.ts shape. Allowlist module: src/core/rules/section-allowlist.ts. CLI command: speckiwi append-note. MCP tool: append_section_note.
- **SHA snapshot guard definition (AC-5)**: the mutation core takes a SHA-256 hash of the target scope file before applying the patch; if the post-read SHA at write time differs from the pre-read SHA (i.e. an external mutation occurred between read and write), the mutation aborts with `STALE_PATCH` rather than silently overwriting. This is the stateless-server equivalent of optimistic-concurrency idempotency.

#### Change Notes

| Date | Change | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-05-16 | Created | add-requirement |
| 2026-05-17 | AC-5 정정: two-call dryRun protocol 제거, optimistic concurrency + SHA snapshot guard 로 명문화 | stateless MCP 환경에서 first-call dryRun 강제는 불가능하며 구현은 SHA snapshot guard 로 동일 의도 달성 |
| 2026-05-16 | Status -> implemented | v2.2.2 implementation + boost cycle 2 complete |
| 2026-05-16 | Status -> verified | AC 8/8 checked, evidence VE-1 attached, 345/345 PASS, A+B축 평가 통과 |

### FR-MCP-019 — set_target_goal mutation and Target Goal read parity

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.2.2 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | mutation, target, governance |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MUST expose a set_target_goal mutation that writes a per-target goal text into the SRS workspace and surface the goal on read paths (summarize_target, get_active_target, list_targets) so agents can consume release intent without a separate tool call.

#### Rationale

Three-agent review (2026-05-16) identified target goal exposure as the highest single UX uplift: agents currently must ask humans for release intent because SSOT has no structured field for it. write+read parity prevents the field from existing without being usable. Architecture-agent rejected adding a column to the Target Map table (breaks parser column indexing) and recommended a Target Goal meta block (see FR-PARSE-018). Governance-agent flagged read-only annotation oversight: set_target_goal MUST be in mutation-tools.ts with readOnlyHint=false.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: AC-1: set_target_goal accepts {target: string, goal: string, dryRun?: boolean}; rejects empty or whitespace-only goal.
- [x] AC-2: AC-2: goal text length limit = 500 UTF-16 code units; control character validation reuses update_status reason policy.
- [x] AC-3: AC-3: target argument MUST exist in the Target Map (00.index.md); nonexistent target returns NOT_FOUND (no auto-create).
- [x] AC-4: AC-4: tool is registered in src/mcp/tools/mutation-tools.ts only with annotations.readOnlyHint = false and kind = workspace per FR-ARCH-005.
- [x] AC-5: AC-5: summarize_target.value.goal exposes the parsed goal for the requested target; absence yields null (not the empty string).
- [x] AC-6: AC-6: get_active_target.value.goal exposes the active target's goal with the same shape.
- [x] AC-7: AC-7: set_target_goal MUST NOT modify any Requirement Block (status, stability, AC, evidence, trace) - verified by automated parity test.
- [x] AC-8: AC-8: an architectural test asserts set_target_goal is NOT registered in src/mcp/tools/read-tools.ts (read-write boundary regression guard).

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP mutation schemas and handlers must expose the same dry-run, notes, diagnostics, and structured mutation-envelope behavior as the shared core mutation services for AC, evidence, trace, requirement, status, stability, target, and completed-work mutations.

#### Rationale

The research found that MCP AC, evidence, and trace mutation adapters can omit dryRun or notes even when the core supports them. Agents that trust MCP previews must not accidentally write files or lose evidence context.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: MCP schemas for check_acceptance_criteria, add_verification_evidence, and add_trace_link accept dryRun when the underlying core mutation supports dry-run behavior.
- [x] AC-2: MCP handlers forward notes and dryRun for evidence and trace mutations and preserve dryRun for AC mutations.
- [x] AC-3: MCP mutation responses expose the shared mutation envelope from FR-NODE-017, including written state, operation preview, diagnostics, and diagnosticsSummary.
- [x] AC-4: MCP failure responses preserve core diagnostics arrays and diagnosticsSummary instead of returning only a code and message.
- [x] AC-5: MCP parity tests prove dry-run calls leave fixture file hashes unchanged and notes round-trip exactly into Verification Evidence and Trace Links rows.

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP must provide low-token requirement discovery through compact list_requirements projections and search_requirements tools with field selection, snippets, filters, pagination, diagnostics, and CLI-equivalent result shapes.

#### Rationale

MCP-first agents need to find the correct Requirement ID without reading whole SRS Markdown. Current MCP list payloads are too heavy and lack search, so agents fall back to file search.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: list_requirements supports compact default projection and explicit full-detail or include-markdown opt-in.
- [x] AC-2: search_requirements returns matching requirement IDs, titles, snippets, file paths, heading lines, target, status, stability, scope, and diagnostics summary.
- [x] AC-3: MCP search filters match the normalized filter set from FR-PARSE-019, including stability, priority, related docs, evidence reference, trace reference, missing evidence, and new-work-candidate state.
- [x] AC-4: MCP discovery responses use the same envelope and field semantics as CLI search and do not include raw Markdown bodies by default.
- [x] AC-5: MCP and CLI parity tests prove equivalent search results for the same fixture query and filters.

### FR-MCP-022 — Diagnostics-preserving MCP envelopes

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | v2.3.0, mcp, diagnostics, errors, envelope |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/09.mcp-cli-tool-abandonment-research.md](../research/09.mcp-cli-tool-abandonment-research.md), [docs/research/10.kiwi-planner-jsonl-tooling-research.md](../research/10.kiwi-planner-jsonl-tooling-research.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP tools and resources must preserve diagnostics, diagnosticsSummary, remediation data, and adapter parity in both success and failure envelopes so MCP clients can recover from validation, lookup, mutation, and workflow-artifact errors without losing context.

#### Rationale

The research found that MCP failure conversion can strip diagnostics down to code and message. MCP-first workflows need the same diagnostic information as CLI and core responses.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: MCP success envelopes include ok, value, diagnostics, diagnosticsSummary, and any relevant metadata without changing the domain-specific value shape unexpectedly.
- [x] AC-2: MCP failure envelopes include ok false, error code, message, diagnostics, diagnosticsSummary, and recoverable next-action hints when available.
- [x] AC-3: MCP mutation failures preserve stale-patch diagnostics and validation diagnostics emitted by core mutation services.
- [x] AC-4: MCP read, resource, mutation, and workflow-artifact tests cover diagnostics-preserving success and failure paths.
- [x] AC-5: MCP envelope behavior remains compatible with existing resource payload policy while documenting any legacy compatibility mode separately.

### ~~REL-MCP-003 — MCP workspace root identity and safety~~ [DISCARDED → see REL-MCP-004]

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | reliability |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | discarded |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | v2.3.0, mcp, workspace, root, safety |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test, inspection |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/09.mcp-cli-tool-abandonment-research.md](../research/09.mcp-cli-tool-abandonment-research.md), [docs/research/10.kiwi-planner-jsonl-tooling-research.md](../research/10.kiwi-planner-jsonl-tooling-research.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP must expose the workspace root it is using, explain the root source, and fail safely when a request would read or mutate the wrong workspace, including multi-root and optional per-call workspace scenarios.

#### Rationale

The research found that MCP root behavior is fragile for agents operating across workspaces. Agents need explicit proof that MCP calls are reading and mutating the intended project root.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: MCP exposes a workspace information tool and includes workspaceRoot, rootSource, index path, active target, package version, and diagnosticsSummary in every read and mutation result envelope, or returns a structured diagnostic when an older envelope cannot carry those fields.
- [x] AC-2: MCP root resolution distinguishes explicit root, server process cwd discovery, auto-init root, and rejected outside-root inputs in machine-readable output.
- [x] AC-3: Optional per-call workspace arguments, if supported, are validated inside the project boundary and fail closed outside the configured root.
- [x] AC-4: Multi-root MCP tests prove tools do not read or mutate a sibling repository when root identity differs from the caller's intended workspace.
- [x] AC-5: MCP docs and managed agent instructions require checking workspace identity before target-scoped reads or mutations in multi-workspace sessions, and tests prove individual tools do not silently omit workspace identity metadata.

#### Verification Evidence

| VE-2 | test | test/mcp/read-tools-resources.test.ts; test/mcp/mutation-tools-errors.test.ts; test/mcp/workflow-read-tools.test.ts; test/mcp/workflow-work-order.test.ts | all | Covers mcp_workspace_info, automatic mcpWorkspace metadata on read and mutation tools, workspaceRoot/rootSource/indexPath/packageVersion reporting, per-call root override fail-closed behavior, read-only workflow tool metadata, and multi-workspace sibling-root rejection. |

### FR-MCP-023 — MCP workflow artifact read tools

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | critical |

| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/10.kiwi-planner-jsonl-tooling-research.md](../research/10.kiwi-planner-jsonl-tooling-research.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP must provide compact workflow-artifact read tools equivalent to CLI workflow reads, including workspace info, artifact discovery, plan status, next-task selection, pipeline status and tailing, session status and resume hints, and worklog tailing through the workflow-read envelope.

#### Rationale

MCP-first agents need workflow state without scanning raw Markdown, sidecar JSON, PM state, pipeline JSONL, and worklog JSONL. MCP must match CLI semantics so skills can use the preferred interface.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: MCP registers read tools for workspace info, artifact listing and resolution, plan status, plan task lookup, next plan task, pipeline status, pipeline tail, pipeline next recommendation, session status, resume hint, and worklog tail.
- [x] AC-2: MCP workflow read tools return the same workflow-read envelope fields and compact defaults as CLI workflow reads.
- [x] AC-3: MCP tools omit raw artifact bodies by default and require explicit includeBody or equivalent input for full bodies.
- [x] AC-4: MCP read tools preserve diagnostics, cursor data, artifact references, hashes or mtimes, and blocking reasons needed for safe resume and next-task decisions.
- [x] AC-5: MCP and CLI parity tests prove equivalent values for the same workflow fixture set, including legacy artifacts, ambiguous artifacts, invalid JSONL, and dependency-blocked next tasks.

### FR-MCP-024 — MCP next work-order tool

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP must provide get_next_work_order or an equivalent compact tool that combines active target, target summary, requirement search, workflow artifacts, pipeline state, PM session state, and worklog tail into the next safe action for MCP-first agents.

#### Rationale

The preferred agent interface is MCP. The next-work-order utility must therefore be available through MCP with the same deterministic behavior as the CLI command.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: MCP exposes get_next_work_order or an equivalent named tool with inputs for explicit target, artifact selection, includeBody opt-in, measurement mode, and ambiguity handling.
- [x] AC-2: The MCP tool returns the same action kinds, required fields, compact defaults, blockers, diagnostics, and payload measurement semantics as the CLI work-order command.
- [x] AC-3: The MCP tool fails closed when workspace identity is ambiguous, active target is unavailable, pipeline state blocks automation, or workflow validators report stale or invalid artifacts.
- [x] AC-4: MCP output includes enough artifact references, hashes or mtimes, requirement IDs, and recommended follow-up tool names for an agent to continue without raw artifact reads.
- [x] AC-5: MCP and CLI work-order parity tests prove equivalent fixture decisions and verify no raw Markdown or raw JSON body appears by default.

### ~~FR-MCP-025 — MCP workflow progress mutation tools~~ [DISCARDED → see FR-MCP-037]

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |

| Status | discarded |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP must register guarded workflow progress mutation tools equivalent to CLI workflow mutation commands so MCP-first agents can update plan display state, PM task status, pipeline and worklog JSONL events, and workflow repair records without direct Markdown or JSON edits.

#### Rationale

The Kiwi planner research makes MCP the preferred agent interface. Without MCP workflow mutation tools, agents cannot safely check task progress, update PM task status, or append workflow JSONL evidence through the official tool surface.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: MCP registers workflow mutation tools for plan task check and uncheck, checklist item checked-state updates, PM task status updates, pipeline JSONL append, worklog JSONL append, and workflow repair record creation.
- [ ] AC-2: MCP workflow mutation schemas expose dryRun, runId, taskId when task-scoped, owner, stale guard inputs, idempotency key inputs, journalKey or canonical journal-key inputs computed by FR-NODE-028, journalState result metadata, and repair metadata needed by the core workflow mutation services.
- [ ] AC-3: MCP handlers forward all workflow mutation fields to core services and return the shared mutation envelope without stripping diagnostics, diagnosticsSummary, operations, stale guards, result, or pendingRepair details.
- [ ] AC-4: MCP workflow mutations fail closed on stale artifacts, forbidden child-coder ownership, invalid JSONL, invalid task IDs, dependency-blocked writes, and partial-failure repair states with structured error or mutation results.
- [ ] AC-5: MCP and CLI parity tests prove equivalent dry-run, no-op, write, stale, forbidden-owner, journal state, partial-failure, and repair-record outcomes for the same workflow mutation fixtures.

### ~~FR-MCP-026 — MCP logical workflow deletion tools~~ [DISCARDED → see FR-MCP-038]

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | discarded |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | v2.3.0, mcp, workflow, jsonl, deletion |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/10.kiwi-planner-jsonl-tooling-research.md](../research/10.kiwi-planner-jsonl-tooling-research.md), [docs/research/11.v2.3.0-tooling-code-improvement-plan.md](../research/11.v2.3.0-tooling-code-improvement-plan.md), [docs/research/230.md](../research/230.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP must expose guarded logical workflow deletion tools that mark supported plan, PM state, pipeline, worklog, and repair records as `deleted` through the shared workflow mutation envelope without physically deleting audit history.

#### Rationale

MCP is the preferred agent interface. Agents need a safe delete-like operation for erroneous workflow records, but the operation must preserve append-only audit and resume evidence by recording a `deleted` state rather than removing source lines.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: MCP registers logical-delete workflow tools or tool actions for supported record classes, including plan display/checklist records, PM task records, pipeline events, worklog events, and workflow repair records when those classes are implemented by core services.
- [ ] AC-2: MCP logical-delete mutation tool schemas expose dryRun, runId, taskId or record identity when applicable, owner, deletion reason, stale guard inputs, and idempotency key inputs; MCP read/list/tail tool schemas expose includeDeleted separately for deleted-record inspection.
- [ ] AC-3: MCP handlers forward all logical-delete fields to core services and return the shared mutation envelope without stripping operations, stale guards, target record metadata, journal state, pendingRepair, diagnostics, or diagnosticsSummary.
- [ ] AC-4: MCP logical-delete tools never physically remove JSONL lines or Markdown history during normal operation; they append or write guarded tombstone/repair state according to FR-NODE-023.
- [ ] AC-5: MCP logical-delete tools treat already-deleted records as successful no-op results with mutation.written false unless the caller explicitly requests an additional repair note.
- [ ] AC-6: MCP and CLI parity tests prove equivalent dry-run, write, no-op, stale, forbidden-owner, invalid JSONL, non-deletable record, includeDeleted read inspection, tombstone/correction-of-tombstone rejection, `status=deleted` rejection, and partial-failure repair outcomes for logical deletion fixtures.

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP list_completed_work and add_completed_work tools must preserve existing tool names and value shapes while supporting external docs/spec/05.completed-work.md storage, source-aware diagnostics, and bounded completed-work reads.

#### Rationale

Agents often use MCP as the normal workflow path. External completed-work storage only reduces raw index reads if the MCP tools expose it safely and keep CLI parity.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: list_completed_work returns completed-work rows from the external log when present and legacy index rows when the external log is absent.
- [ ] AC-2: add_completed_work writes through the FR-NODE-024 canonical target resolver and reports the selected target file through response metadata, patch summary, or diagnostics.
- [ ] AC-3: MCP completed-work responses preserve existing row fields and support CLI-equivalent bounded read inputs for limit and ordering, returning total count, returned count, and hasMore or continuation metadata when rows are omitted.
- [ ] AC-4: MCP diagnostics identify whether completed-work rows came from docs/spec/00.index.md or docs/spec/05.completed-work.md in duplicate-source or migration-warning scenarios.
- [ ] AC-5: MCP and CLI parity tests prove equivalent completed-work reads and writes for external-only, legacy-only, and dual-source fixtures.

### ~~FR-MCP-028 — MCP SRS mutation lock controls~~ [DISCARDED → see FR-MCP-032]

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |

| Status | discarded |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | v2.3.0, srs-lock, mcp, ignore-lock, CAND-NODE-SRS-MUTATION-LOCK |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/230.md](../research/230.md), [docs/research/11.v2.3.0-tooling-code-improvement-plan.md](../research/11.v2.3.0-tooling-code-improvement-plan.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP mutation tools that can write SRS files must honor the SRS mutation lock, return structured SRS_LOCKED responses when locked, and expose an explicit ignoreLock input that bypasses only the SRS lock.

#### Rationale

In stdio MCP, process-local memory is not enough to coordinate SRS writes across clients or restarts. MCP needs the same lock and escape-hatch semantics as CLI while preserving read-only operation.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: All MCP tools that can write docs/spec, docs/rule, SRS index rollups, or SRS mutation cache files check the REL-NODE-004 lock before writing.
- [ ] AC-2: When a non-stale lock is active, MCP mutation tools fail without writing and return an error envelope containing code SRS_LOCKED, owner, operation, request ID, acquiredAt, expiresAt, diagnostics, and retry guidance.
- [ ] AC-3: The ignoreLock input is accepted only by SRS mutation tools and is not accepted or interpreted by read-only MCP tools.
- [ ] AC-4: Using ignoreLock true records a diagnostic or response metadata entry indicating that the lock was ignored.
- [ ] AC-5: ignoreLock true does not bypass stale patch checks, generated-ID collision checks, table-cell safety, trace validation, SRS validation, workflow stale guards, or non-SRS locks.
- [ ] AC-6: MCP tests cover active lock denial, stale lock recovery, ignored-lock write path, read-only tool behavior under lock, and CLI/MCP parity for lock diagnostics.

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP completed-work resources must preserve speckiwi://completed-work and speckiwi://completed-work/{target} compatibility while serving external docs/spec/05.completed-work.md rows, legacy index rows, source-aware diagnostics, reportPaths arrays, and bounded payload metadata through the same completed-work source model as MCP tools.

#### Rationale

Doc230 requires completed-work MCP tool names, resource URIs, and JSON payload shape to remain compatible. FR-MCP-027 covers tools; the resource URIs need an explicit contract so agents do not regress to raw index reads.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: speckiwi://completed-work returns completed-work rows from docs/spec/05.completed-work.md when the external log exists and from the legacy index table when the external log is absent.
- [ ] AC-2: speckiwi://completed-work/{target} applies the same target filtering semantics as list_completed_work and the CLI completed-work command.
- [ ] AC-3: Completed-work resources preserve existing value.completedWork row fields, including reportPaths arrays, and add source or bounded-payload metadata only through backward-compatible envelope metadata or optional fields.
- [ ] AC-4: Completed-work resources expose duplicate-source and migration diagnostics that identify whether rows came from docs/spec/00.index.md or docs/spec/05.completed-work.md.
- [ ] AC-5: Completed-work resources use the same bounded payload policy as FR-MCP-027 and do not return long completed-work history by default when a caller requests bounded output.
- [ ] AC-6: MCP resource tests prove tool/resource parity for external-only, legacy-only, target-filtered, bounded, and dual-source diagnostic fixtures.

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP must expose Completed Work Log read and append tools that support the external docs/spec/05.completed-work.md source, preserve legacy index behavior, and return source-selection diagnostics in the same shape as CLI completed-work operations.

#### Rationale

Doc230 found that agents often need completed-work context without loading the full index. MCP read and mutation tools need first-class external-log support so stdio agents can query and append completed work without manual Markdown edits.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: MCP completed-work read tools return entries from the authoritative completed-work source with source file, source line, total count, returned count, hasMore, and continuation metadata.
- [x] AC-2: MCP add_completed_work writes to docs/spec/05.completed-work.md when that file is present and falls back to docs/spec/00.index.md only for legacy workspaces without the external file.
- [x] AC-3: MCP completed-work read and append responses are CLI-equivalent for limit, ordering, reportPaths, diagnostics, and duplicate-source handling.
- [x] AC-4: If both index and external completed-work rows are non-empty, MCP read responses include the duplicate-source or migration diagnostic and MCP write responses fail closed unless the core source-selection policy explicitly permits the write.
- [x] AC-5: MCP tests cover external-only, legacy-only, dual-source diagnostic, append-to-external, append-to-legacy, pagination metadata, malformed report paths, and dry-run append behavior.

SpecKiwi MCP resources must expose bounded Completed Work Log resource views backed by docs/spec/05.completed-work.md when present, while preserving legacy index resource compatibility and surfacing source-selection diagnostics.

### FR-MCP-032 — MCP SRS mutation lock controls

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | v2.3.0, srs-lock, ignore-lock, mcp, concurrency, CAND-NODE-SRS-MUTATION-LOCK |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/230.md](../research/230.md), [docs/research/11.v2.3.0-tooling-code-improvement-plan.md](../research/11.v2.3.0-tooling-code-improvement-plan.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP SRS mutation tools must honor the SRS mutation lock, return structured SRS_LOCKED diagnostics, and expose a narrow ignoreLock boolean that bypasses only the SRS lock while preserving all other mutation safety checks.

#### Rationale

Doc230 found that stdio MCP clients can create concurrent mutation attempts and need machine-readable retry guidance. MCP must expose the same SRS lock semantics as CLI without turning ignoreLock into a general unsafe mode.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: All MCP tools that can mutate docs/spec, docs/rule as part of SRS mutation contracts, SRS index rollups, or SRS mutation cache files acquire the shared SRS lock before writing.
- [x] AC-2: When the lock is active and non-stale, MCP mutations fail before editing files with ok false, error.code SRS_LOCKED, diagnostics, diagnosticsSummary, owner, operation, requestId, acquiredAt, expiresAt, and retry guidance.
- [x] AC-3: MCP exposes ignoreLock only on SRS mutation tool schemas, records diagnostics when it is used, and never lets the flag bypass stale patch guards, generated-ID collision checks, table-cell safety, trace validation, or SRS validation.
- [x] AC-4: MCP dryRun calls report whether a real mutation would be lock-blocked without modifying lock state or SRS files.
- [x] AC-5: MCP tests cover active lock denial, stale lock recovery, ignoreLock diagnostics, dryRun lock preview, and preservation of existing mutation safety failures under ignoreLock.

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP must expose workflow diagnostic projection tools, work-order explain and context profiles, and an official pipeline event emit surface that delegate to the same core contracts and result shapes as the CLI workflow projection commands.

#### Rationale

Doc230 Plus found that MCP-first agents need the same low-token workflow projections and guarded JSONL append behavior as CLI users. MCP tool names and schema options must be explicit so agents do not fall back to raw Markdown or shell JSONL edits.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: MCP registers read-only projection tools or equivalent named operations for workflow diagnosis, normalized workflow diff, workflow schema-check, pipeline status, pipeline tail, and optional pipeline compact behavior, all returning compact envelopes without raw bodies by default.
- [ ] AC-2: MCP get_next_work_order or its equivalent accepts explain and compact context profile options that return decisionTrace, rejected candidates, blockers, artifact references, source hashes or mtimes, and diagnostics generated by the work-order engine.
- [ ] AC-3: MCP registers emit_pipeline_event or an equivalent official pipeline append tool only as a wrapper over shared JSONL append and guarded workflow mutation services, with schema validation, parsed skill and run_id dedupe, trailing LF enforcement, dryRun support, and shared mutation-envelope output.
- [ ] AC-4: MCP projection and profile tools fail closed on invalid artifacts, unsupported schema versions, dependency blockers, active FAILED or NEEDS_USER pipeline state, ambiguous workspace identity, and ambiguous target state.
- [ ] AC-5: MCP and CLI parity tests prove equivalent projection decisions, work-order explain output, compact context output, pipeline emit mutation behavior, diagnostics, and raw-body omission for the same fixtures.

### FR-MCP-034 — MCP read-only legacy workflow migration preview

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | v2.3.0, mcp, workflow, migration-preview, legacy |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/230plus.md](../research/230plus.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP must expose a read-only legacy workflow migration preview tool that delegates to the core migration-preview service and returns diagnostics-preserving compact output without applying, writing, moving, deleting, or normalizing workflow artifacts.

#### Rationale

Doc230 Plus recommends read-only legacy migration preview as a future diagnostic. MCP-first agents need this preview through a safe tool surface instead of scanning legacy directories and inferring migration actions manually.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: MCP registers preview_workflow_migration, preview_legacy_workflow_migration, or an equivalent documented tool that returns the FR-NODE-029 preview envelope.
- [x] AC-2: The tool reports legacy source paths, confidence, source hashes, proposed destinations, unsupported fields, data-loss risks, target drift, path drift, diagnostics, and diagnosticsSummary without raw bodies by default.
- [x] AC-3: The tool always reports written false and does not create, update, move, delete, normalize, compact, or append any workflow, pipeline, plan, session, worklog, or SRS file.
- [x] AC-4: Any input that requests apply, write, fix, normalize, or migrate behavior fails with a structured unsupported_operation diagnostic unless a later migration-apply requirement exists.
- [x] AC-5: MCP and CLI parity tests cover legacy-only preview, mixed current and legacy artifacts, includeBody opt-in, unsupported apply requests, malformed legacy artifacts, target drift, and no-write file hash preservation.

### FR-MCP-035 — MCP workflow read projections and work-order profiles

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | critical |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP must expose read-only workflow diagnostic projection tools and work-order output profiles that delegate to shared workflow read, validator, and work-order services without introducing duplicate selectors or mutation behavior.

#### Rationale

This requirement supersedes the read/profile portion of FR-MCP-033 and removes emit_pipeline_event mutation naming from the read projection contract. Doc230 Plus recommends MCP read projections and context packing as compact read-only or work-order views.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: MCP registers read-only projection tools or equivalent named operations for workflow diagnosis, normalized workflow diff, workflow schema-check, pipeline status, pipeline tail, and optional pipeline compact behavior, all returning compact envelopes without raw bodies by default.
- [x] AC-2: MCP get_next_work_order or its equivalent accepts explain and compact context profile options that return decisionTrace, rejected candidates, blockers, artifact references, source hashes or mtimes, and diagnostics generated by the work-order engine.
- [x] AC-3: MCP projection and profile tools fail closed on invalid artifacts, unsupported schema versions, dependency blockers, active FAILED or NEEDS_USER pipeline state, ambiguous workspace identity, and ambiguous target state.
- [x] AC-4: MCP and CLI parity tests prove equivalent projection decisions, work-order explain output, compact context output, diagnostics, and raw-body omission for the same fixtures.
- [x] AC-5: This requirement does not define any mutating pipeline emit behavior; official JSONL append tool naming belongs to the separate MCP mutation requirement that depends on FR-NODE-030.

### ~~FR-MCP-036 — MCP workflow mutation tools and pipeline emit~~ [DISCARDED → see FR-MCP-037]

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |

| Stability | evolving |

| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/10.kiwi-planner-jsonl-tooling-research.md](../research/10.kiwi-planner-jsonl-tooling-research.md), [docs/research/09.mcp-cli-tool-abandonment-research.md](../research/09.mcp-cli-tool-abandonment-research.md), [docs/research/230plus.md](../research/230plus.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP must register guarded workflow progress mutation tools and an official pipeline event emit tool using the shared mutation envelope, the FR-NODE-028 canonical journal identity, and the FR-NODE-030 guarded mutation core services.

#### Rationale

This requirement supersedes FR-MCP-025 to correct journalKey semantics and takes the mutating emit_pipeline_event surface out of FR-MCP-033. MCP mutation naming must be separate from read-only projections.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: MCP registers workflow mutation tools for plan task check and uncheck, checklist item checked-state updates, PM task status updates, pipeline JSONL append, worklog JSONL append, and workflow repair record creation.
- [ ] AC-2: MCP workflow mutation schemas expose dryRun, runId, taskId when task-scoped, owner, stale guard inputs, idempotency key inputs, journalKey or canonical journal-key inputs computed exactly by FR-NODE-028, journalState result metadata, and repair metadata needed by the core workflow mutation services.
- [ ] AC-3: MCP handlers forward all workflow mutation fields to core services and return the shared mutation envelope without stripping diagnostics, diagnosticsSummary, operations, stale guards, result, or pendingRepair details.
- [ ] AC-4: MCP workflow mutations fail closed on stale artifacts, forbidden child-coder ownership, invalid JSONL, invalid task IDs, dependency-blocked writes, and partial-failure repair states with structured error or mutation results.
- [ ] AC-5: MCP registers emit_pipeline_event or an equivalent official pipeline append tool that validates schema, resolves the canonical pipeline path, serializes one JSON object per line, enforces trailing LF, dedupes by parsed skill and run_id, supports dryRun, and returns the shared mutation envelope.
- [ ] AC-6: MCP and CLI parity tests prove equivalent dry-run, no-op, write, stale, forbidden-owner, journal state, partial-failure, repair-record, and pipeline emit parsed-dedupe outcomes for the same workflow mutation fixtures.

### FR-MCP-038 — MCP logical workflow deletion with canonical mutation dependency

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | v2.3.0, mcp, workflow, jsonl, deletion |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/10.kiwi-planner-jsonl-tooling-research.md](../research/10.kiwi-planner-jsonl-tooling-research.md), [docs/research/11.v2.3.0-tooling-code-improvement-plan.md](../research/11.v2.3.0-tooling-code-improvement-plan.md), [docs/research/230.md](../research/230.md), [docs/research/230plus.md](../research/230plus.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP must expose guarded logical workflow deletion tools that mark supported plan, PM state, pipeline, worklog, and repair records as `deleted` through the shared workflow mutation envelope without physically deleting audit history, using the canonical FR-MCP-037 mutation tool contract.

#### Rationale

This requirement supersedes FR-MCP-026 so active MCP logical-delete work no longer extends the discarded FR-MCP-025 mutation contract. The behavior remains the same, but the dependency is now the canonical Doc230 Plus MCP mutation successor.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: MCP registers logical-delete workflow tools or tool actions for supported record classes, including plan display/checklist records, PM task records, pipeline events, worklog events, and workflow repair records when those classes are implemented by core services.
- [x] AC-2: MCP logical-delete mutation tool schemas expose dryRun, runId, taskId or record identity when applicable, owner, deletion reason, stale guard inputs, and idempotency key inputs; MCP read/list/tail tool schemas expose includeDeleted separately for deleted-record inspection.
- [x] AC-3: MCP handlers forward all logical-delete fields to core services and return the shared mutation envelope without stripping operations, stale guards, target record metadata, journal state, pendingRepair, diagnostics, or diagnosticsSummary.
- [x] AC-4: MCP logical-delete tools never physically remove JSONL lines or Markdown history during normal operation; they append or write guarded tombstone/repair state according to FR-NODE-031.
- [x] AC-5: MCP logical-delete tools treat already-deleted records as successful no-op results with mutation.written false unless the caller explicitly requests an additional repair note.
- [x] AC-6: MCP and CLI parity tests prove equivalent dry-run, write, no-op, stale, forbidden-owner, invalid JSONL, non-deletable record, includeDeleted read inspection, tombstone/correction-of-tombstone rejection, status=deleted rejection, and partial-failure repair outcomes for logical deletion fixtures.

### FR-MCP-039 — MCP Requirement ID collision repair tools

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | v2.3.0, mcp, requirement-id, collision-repair, merge |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [docs/research/230.md](../research/230.md) |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP must expose guarded Requirement ID collision diagnosis, planning, and application tools that delegate to the FR-NODE-032 core service and preserve CLI-equivalent structured diagnostics for merge-time duplicate Requirement ID repair.

#### Rationale

Doc230 found that MCP-first agents need a repair tool because duplicate IDs make read and mutation lookup ambiguous. The MCP adapter must not implement repair logic independently or lose core diagnostics through result conversion.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: MCP registers diagnose_requirement_id_collisions, plan_requirement_id_collision_repair, and apply_requirement_id_collision_repair tools or documented equivalent names. Diagnose and plan schemas include duplicateId, occurrence identity, replacementId or allocation strategy, reference policy, explicit reference edits, and dryRun but reject ignoreLock with diagnostics; only the apply schema may include ignoreLock.
- [x] AC-2: The diagnose and plan tools are no-write by default and return grouped SRS-E002 diagnostics, occurrence identity, generated replacement ID candidates, ambiguous inbound references, provenance hints, and patch preview when a plan is requested.
- [x] AC-3: The apply tool requires explicit occurrence mapping or a prior plan identity, delegates all writes to FR-NODE-032, and returns the shared mutation envelope without stripping diagnostics, diagnosticsSummary, operations, stale guards, lock state, generated-ID collision detail, or pendingRepair metadata.
- [x] AC-4: MCP failures return structured tool results for SRS_LOCKED, AMBIGUOUS_REQUIREMENT_DUPLICATE, AMBIGUOUS_REFERENCE, STALE_PATCH, GENERATED_ID_COLLISION, unresolved Git conflict markers, and PARTIAL_MUTATION_FAILURE instead of protocol-level errors for recoverable repair states.
- [x] AC-5: ignoreLock bypasses only the SRS mutation lock and never bypasses stale patch checks, generated-ID collision checks, table-cell safety, trace validation, SRS validation, or explicit occurrence identity checks.
- [x] AC-6: MCP and CLI parity tests prove equivalent dry-run, no-op, write, stale, lock, ambiguous-reference, generated-ID collision, partial-failure, and validation-clean outcomes for the same repair fixtures.

### ~~REL-MCP-004 — MCP workspace root identity and safety (cwd-only)~~ [DISCARDED → see REL-MCP-005]

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | reliability |
| Target | v2.3.0 |
| Status | discarded |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | v2.3.0, mcp, workspace, root, safety, user-directive |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test, inspection |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP는 사용 중인 workspace root와 root 소스를 노출하고, explicit root 파라미터 없이 server process cwd discovery와 auto-init만으로 root를 해석하며, 잘못된 workspace를 읽거나 변이하는 요청에 대해 fail-closed 해야 한다. MCP 서버 기동·도구 호출 어느 표면에도 root 파라미터가 존재하지 않아야 한다.

#### Rationale

사용자 지시(2026-07-06)로 MCP의 root 파라미터를 전면 제거한다. REL-MCP-003이 보장하던 workspace 식별·multi-root 안전성·per-call fail-closed를 계승하되, rootSource의 explicit 분기를 제거하여 "서버가 뜬 곳 = 작업 대상"으로 단순화하고 잘못된 root 기동으로 인한 조용한 오염 경로를 차단한다.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: MCP는 workspace information tool을 노출하고 모든 read/mutation 결과 envelope에 workspaceRoot, rootSource, index path, active target, package version, diagnosticsSummary를 포함한다.
- [x] AC-2: rootSource는 server-cwd-discovery 또는 auto-init만 보고하며 explicit 값은 존재하지 않는다.
- [x] AC-3: per-call root/workspaceRoot 인자는 지원하지 않으며 구조화된 진단과 함께 fail-closed 거부한다.
- [x] AC-4: startMcpServer/McpServerOptions는 root 파라미터를 노출하지 않고, MCP 기동 root 해석은 cwd 상위 탐색과 auto-init 경로만 갖는다.
- [x] AC-5: Multi-root 테스트가 호출자의 의도 workspace와 root identity가 다를 때 sibling repository를 읽거나 변이하지 않음을 증명한다(테스트 전용 내부 DI seam 사용 허용).
- [x] AC-6: MCP 문서와 managed agent instructions는 multi-workspace 세션에서 target-scoped read/mutation 전 workspace identity 확인을 요구하며, 테스트가 개별 tool의 workspace identity metadata 누락이 없음을 증명한다.

SRS 계열은 호스트에 고정한 채로 두므로 다중 root 를 5회 거절시킨 요구 ID 충돌은 구조적으로 발생할 수 없다. |

### FR-MCP-040 — MCP validate_step tool registration

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL register a read-only MCP tool named `validate_step` that accepts a step name and runs the step-local validation pass (validateWorkspaceScoped) over the parsed workspace, returning that step's diagnostics in the standard split read envelope ({ errors, warnings }). Returned diagnostics SHALL be scoped to docs/spec/steps/<step>/ so that a body-scope diagnostic never leaks into the step result. The tool SHALL surface the step advisory rules SRS-W044 (a step requirement shadows a body requirement id) and SRS-W045 (step overload) when present, and SHALL emit STEP_* same-requirement conflict advisories only as warnings, never as gate-failing errors. The tool SHALL be projected from the single ToolSpec command-metadata registry so the registry-derived MCP surfaces (tool names, tool schemas, and the read-only tool set) all include it without drift.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Calling `validate_step` with a step name returns an ok envelope whose `errors` and `warnings` are arrays holding only diagnostics scoped to that step; a genuine body-scope structural error (e.g. a broken index Scope Map heading, code SRS-E014) does not appear in the returned diagnostics.
- [ ] AC-2: When the named step reuses a body requirement id and reaches the step-overload threshold, the returned diagnostics include both SRS-W044 (step-shadows-body) and SRS-W045 (step-overload).
- [ ] AC-3: At least one STEP_* advisory is surfaced among the `warnings`, and no STEP_* code ever appears among the gate-failing `errors`.
- [ ] AC-4: AC-1 (registry projection facet): `validate_step` is a read-only tool projected from the ToolSpec registry — it appears in renderToolNames(), in the keys of renderToolSchemas(), and in renderReadOnlyToolNames().

### FR-MCP-041 — MCP registration of compatibility-check mutation tools and compatibility edge read tools

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL expose the v3 compatibility-check surface through the MCP server by registering three compatibility mutation tools — add_compatibility_check, refresh_compatibility_check, and revoke_compatibility_check — and two compatibility edge read tools — list_dirty_edges and list_compat_edges. Each mutation tool SHALL be declared in the ToolSpec registry with a zod input schema accepting the two requirement endpoints (aReqId, bReqId) plus an optional dryRun flag, SHALL be classified as a non-read-only mutation tool, and SHALL forward its endpoint inputs unchanged to the corresponding core mutation (addCompatibilityCheck / refreshCompatibilityCheck / revokeCompatibilityCheck, per FR-NODE-022 / FR-NODE-023). Each edge read tool SHALL be registered as a read-only tool that projects the listDirtyEdges core read. Every registered tool SHALL return diagnostics-preserving MCP result envelopes, and the addition of these tools SHALL keep the zero-drift tool-surface contract (name, schema, and kind consistency across every derived surface) intact.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: add_compatibility_check, refresh_compatibility_check, and revoke_compatibility_check are registered on the running MCP server and appear in the ToolSpec name view; each renders a zod input schema whose aReqId and bReqId arguments are zod schemas; each is classified as a mutation kind (req-scoped / workspace / log-append) with isReadOnlyTool returning false; and assertZeroDriftToolSurface() does not throw with these tools registered.
- [ ] AC-2: list_dirty_edges and list_compat_edges are registered on the running MCP server and appear in the ToolSpec name view; each carries the 'read' kind, is present in the read-only tool set, and isReadOnlyTool returns true; and assertZeroDriftToolSurface() does not throw with these tools registered.
- [ ] AC-3: Against a real workspace with two live requirements (no mocks), calling add_compatibility_check forwards {aReqId, bReqId} to the core mutation and writes exactly one checked_compatible trace row on the minimum requirement-ID block referencing the peer; refresh_compatibility_check replaces that single row in place (same holder and reference, canonical pin grammar); revoke_compatibility_check removes the row entirely; a subsequent revoke of the now-missing edge returns {ok:false, error:{code:'NOT_FOUND'}}; and add_compatibility_check with a nonexistent peer endpoint returns {ok:false, error:{code:'NOT_FOUND'}}, proving inputs reach the core liveness guard rather than a stub.

### FR-MCP-042 — MCP registration of step state tools

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL register claim_step, update_step_state, and list_steps as MCP tools, each exposing a zod input schema derived from the ToolSpec registry. claim_step and update_step_state SHALL be classified as mutation tools (not read-only), while list_steps SHALL be classified as a read-only tool that never writes step state. Each tool handler SHALL forward its inputs to the corresponding core implementation — claimStep (FR-NODE-027), updateStepState (FR-NODE-028), and listSteps (FR-NODE-029) — rather than a fixed stub, so that the core guards, error codes, and results are observable through the MCP surface. Registration of these tools SHALL preserve the zero-drift tool-surface contract across every derived registry view.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: claim_step, update_step_state, and list_steps are each registered on the running MCP server and appear in the registry name view (renderToolNames), expose a rendered zod input schema whose `step` argument is a zod schema, and carry a declared tool kind from the registry kind set (read, req-scoped, workspace, log-append). claim_step and update_step_state resolve to mutation kinds and are excluded from the read-only set (isReadOnlyTool returns false); list_steps has kind "read" and is in the read-only set (isReadOnlyTool returns true). The zero-drift tool-surface assertion (assertZeroDriftToolSurface) still passes with the three tools registered.
- [ ] AC-2: Each step-state tool forwards to its core implementation, exercising the real core behavior (not a stub): (a) claim_step invokes claimStep — an unconflicted claim appends a new active state.md row carrying the declared TouchesScope and TouchesReq and returns ok:true, while a claim whose TouchesReq directly intersects an active step's TouchesReq returns ok:false with error code STEP_DIRECT_CONFLICT and leaves state.md byte-for-byte unchanged; (b) update_step_state invokes updateStepState — a valid status change rewrites the target row's Status cell in place and returns ok:true, an out-of-enum status returns ok:false with error code INVALID_STATUS, and updating a non-existent step returns ok:false with error code NOT_FOUND; (c) list_steps invokes listSteps — it returns ok:true with steps ordered by Kahn topological sort honoring DependsOn edges (a step ordered before its dependents) and cycle:false for an acyclic graph.

| Field | Value |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL register two SRS mutation tools on the MCP server surface — supersede_requirement and promote_step_requirement — each exposing a zod input schema and forwarding to its corresponding core mutation (supersedeRequirement / FR-NODE-030 and promoteStepRequirement / FR-NODE-031) without weakening those mutations' guards. Both tools SHALL be classified as mutation (non-read-only) tools and SHALL appear consistently across every derived registry view (tool names, schemas, kinds). Registering these tools SHALL preserve the zero-drift tool-surface contract (FR-ARCH-006 / REL-ARCH-002) so that no derived surface diverges.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: supersede_requirement is registered on the running MCP server and appears in the derived tool-name, schema, and kind views; its rendered zod input schema exposes oldId, scope, target, title, statement, and acceptanceCriteria as zod schemas; it is a non-read-only mutation tool (kind req-scoped/workspace/log-append) and the zero-drift tool-surface assertion still passes; invoking it forwards to core supersedeRequirement (FR-NODE-030), minting a distinct successor id (returned as newId, not equal to oldId) that carries a Requirement supersedes <oldId> trace row while the old requirement becomes discarded; and a successor pinned to oldId is rejected with error code MUTATION_DENIED, proving forwarding rather than a fixed-ok stub.
- [ ] AC-2: promote_step_requirement is registered on the running MCP server and appears in the derived tool-name, schema, and kind views; its rendered zod input schema exposes id, fromStep, and toScope as zod schemas; it is a non-read-only mutation tool (kind req-scoped/workspace/log-append) and the zero-drift tool-surface assertion still passes; invoking it forwards to core promoteStepRequirement (FR-NODE-031), inserting the step's pre-minted id verbatim into the target body scope (returned as requirementId and present as a body requirement heading) whereas before promotion the id existed only in the step origin; and an id colliding with an existing body requirement is rejected (ok:false), proving forwarding rather than a fixed-ok stub.

### FR-MCP-045 — MCP set_work_mode and get_work_mode work-mode tools

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL expose MCP tools set_work_mode and get_work_mode that read and switch the project work Mode (one of sdd, vibe, wait) persisted in the docs/spec/steps/state.md metadata block, preserving parity with the CLI `mode` command and the shared core work-mode model. get_work_mode SHALL return the current Mode and, when Mode is vibe, the Active Task name, and SHALL fail open to Mode=wait (without error) when state.md is absent or its Mode value is invalid. set_work_mode SHALL switch Mode to a valid enum value and persist it — persisting the Active Task name when switching to vibe and clearing any stale Active Task line when switching to a non-vibe mode — while rejecting an invalid Mode value with a structured error instead of corrupting the persisted state.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: get_work_mode returns the persisted current work Mode; when Mode is vibe it also returns the Active Task name (Active Task is absent for sdd/wait).
- [ ] AC-2: get_work_mode fails open to Mode=wait, without throwing or erroring, when docs/spec/steps/state.md is absent or its Mode value falls outside {sdd, vibe, wait}.
- [ ] AC-3: set_work_mode switches Mode to any of sdd, vibe, or wait and persists it into the state.md metadata block, so that a subsequent get_work_mode reflects the switched Mode.
- [ ] AC-4: set_work_mode to vibe persists the Active Task name alongside Mode=vibe; switching to a non-vibe mode (sdd or wait) removes any stale Active Task line so state.md stays self-consistent for the vibe-gate.
- [ ] AC-5: set_work_mode rejects an invalid Mode value with a structured (ok=false) error and leaves the previously persisted valid Mode intact rather than writing the invalid value.

| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide an MCP `start_vibe_task` tool that begins a vibe-mode work task from a supplied implementation intent. The tool SHALL accept an explicit task name, or derive a task-name slug from the intent when auto-derive is requested. It SHALL write the task's `intent.md` before setting `Mode=vibe` and the Active Task in the step-state, guaranteeing intent-first ordering so that a crash between the two writes can never leave an Active Task without a corresponding `intent.md`. When the resolved task name collides with an existing task, the system SHALL disambiguate it by appending a suffix.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: start_vibe_task writes the task `intent.md` before it sets the Active Task, so an interrupted call never yields an Active Task lacking an intent.md (intent-first ordering).
- [ ] AC-2: start_vibe_task accepts an explicit task name, or derives a task-name slug from the supplied intent when auto-derive is requested.
- [ ] AC-3: On successful completion, the persisted work mode is `vibe` and the Active Task equals the resolved task name.
- [ ] AC-4: A duplicate (already-existing) task name is disambiguated by appending a suffix to make it unique.

#### Requirement

The SpecKiwi MCP server SHALL register a `retarget_requirements` tool that reassigns a caller-selected set of Requirement IDs to a single destination Target through the shared retarget core mutation (FR-NODE-047). The tool SHALL default to a dry-run preview that writes no SRS file, and SHALL return a per-item result list containing exactly one entry for each input Requirement ID, where each entry carries either the applied move (fromTarget → toTarget) or a `skipReason`. The tool input schema SHALL accept neither a `status` field nor an active-target field, so the tool can neither finalize requirements nor change the Active Target.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: `retarget_requirements` is a registered MCP tool whose input schema accepts an array of Requirement IDs and a single destination Target, and exposes no `status` field and no active-target field.
- [ ] AC-2: When the `dryRun` argument is omitted, the tool performs a preview only and writes no SRS file.
- [ ] AC-3: The tool result contains exactly one entry per input Requirement ID, each carrying either an applied move (fromTarget/toTarget) or a `skipReason` value drawn from {excluded, not-found, target-not-registered, frozen-needs-change-note}.
- [ ] AC-4: A Requirement ID whose destination Target is not registered in the index Target Map (docs/spec/00.index.md) yields a `skipReason` of `target-not-registered` and its Target metadata is left unchanged.
- [ ] AC-5: The tool schema accepts no `status` field and no active-target field; a frozen (SRS-MD §16 rule 5) requirement that is moved with a supplied reason records that reason into its Change Notes, while moving a frozen requirement without a reason instead yields a `skipReason` of `frozen-needs-change-note`.
