### FR-MCP-048 — MCP update_requirement_field mutation tool with id-regeneration guard

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The SpecKiwi MCP server SHALL register an update_requirement_field mutation tool that edits a single editable metadata field of one Requirement through the shared field-update core. The tool SHALL accept exactly one Requirement ID and one field name, and SHALL apply the edit as a single-line replacement for line-replacement fields. When the edited field is type or scope, the tool SHALL treat the edit as an ID-regenerating migration: it SHALL default to a dry-run preview that reports the old ID, the regenerated new ID, and the planned move without writing, and SHALL require an explicit sign-off before writing. A confirmed type or scope migration SHALL regenerate the Requirement ID via the deterministic prefix-scope-NNN generator (never a caller-supplied ID) and rewrite inbound Trace Link references from the old ID to the new ID.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: update_requirement_field is a registered MCP mutation tool that accepts exactly one Requirement ID and one field name (single-requirement scope, not a batch).
- [ ] AC-2: The accepted field set is priority, risk, title, target, verification-method, type, and scope; any other field name is rejected with a structured usage error and no file is written.
- [ ] AC-3: A type or scope edit defaults to a dry-run preview that returns the old ID, the regenerated new ID, and the planned block move, and writes no file when no explicit sign-off is supplied.
- [ ] AC-4: A type or scope write attempted without an explicit sign-off flag returns a structured error and writes no file; a confirmed type or scope edit regenerates the Requirement ID using the deterministic prefix-scope-NNN generator rather than accepting a caller-supplied ID, producing a new non-colliding ID and rewriting inbound Trace Link references from the old ID to the new ID.
- [ ] AC-5: The update_requirement_field schema rejects an array of Requirement IDs at the schema layer, enforcing single-requirement scope.

| Field | Value |

#### Requirement

The SpecKiwi MCP server SHALL register two req-scoped mutation tools — `add_related_doc` and `add_change_note` — that each target a single Requirement by ID and forward to a shared core mutation. `add_related_doc` SHALL append one document reference to the target requirement's Related Docs metadata value (replacing the `-` placeholder or comma-appending to an existing value). `add_change_note` SHALL append one dated row (date, change, reason) to the target requirement's Change Notes table while preserving existing rows. Both tools SHALL leave every other section of the requirement unchanged, support a dry-run that previews the patch without writing the file, and be exposed only on the mutation surface (never as read tools).

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: `add_related_doc` is a registered req-scoped MCP mutation tool that accepts one Requirement ID and one document reference and appends that reference to the requirement's Related Docs field (replacing the `-` placeholder or comma-appending), leaving all other sections byte-for-byte unchanged.
- [ ] AC-2: `add_change_note` is a registered req-scoped MCP mutation tool that accepts one Requirement ID plus change and reason text and appends a single dated row to the requirement's Change Notes table while preserving all existing rows.
- [ ] AC-3: Both tools reject an array of Requirement IDs at the input-schema layer (the `id` input is a single string, not a list).
- [ ] AC-4: When no explicit date is supplied, `add_change_note` prepends an ISO calendar date (YYYY-MM-DD) as the first cell of the appended Change Notes row.
- [ ] AC-5: Both tools are registered on the MCP mutation surface and are absent from the read-tools surface, each forwarding to its shared core mutation (addRelatedDoc / addChangeNote) and supporting a dry-run that returns a patch preview without writing.

| Field | Value |

#### Requirement

The SpecKiwi MCP server SHALL register a read-only `summarize_release_readiness` tool that projects the existing release-readiness core summary for a target without mutating any file or creating git tags. The tool SHALL accept an optional target and default to the Active Target when the target is omitted, while an explicitly supplied target overrides the Active Target. The returned payload SHALL surface the release-readiness core module's per-status and per-stability requirement groupings, the readiness blockers, and an overall ready/not-ready verdict for the resolved target, by delegating to the existing core computation rather than recomputing readiness inline.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: `summarize_release_readiness` is registered as an MCP read-only tool and invoking it performs no file mutation and creates no git tag (the payload only reports the baseline command as text).
- [ ] AC-2: The tool accepts an optional `target` argument; when omitted it resolves to the Active Target (targetSource=active-target), when supplied the explicit target wins over the Active Target (targetSource=explicit), and an empty resolved target yields ready=false with a warning that the release target is empty.
- [ ] AC-3: The returned payload groups requirement identifiers by status (blocked, planned/in-progress, implemented-not-verified, missing-evidence) and by stability (draft/stability-blockers, deprecated, stability-warnings) for the resolved target, keeping stability blockers/warnings separate from status blockers.
- [ ] AC-4: The payload reports an overall readiness verdict (ready) that is false whenever any status blocker, stability blocker, missing-evidence entry, acceptance-criteria coverage gap, broken trace link, evidence-reference issue, command-evidence-policy violation, or validation error exists, or the target is empty.
- [ ] AC-5: The tool delegates to the existing release-readiness core module (`summarizeReleaseReadiness` in src/core/workflow/release-readiness.ts) rather than recomputing readiness inline.

### FR-MCP-051 — explain_diagnostic read tool for SRS diagnostic-code definitions

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.3.0.1 |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | low |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The system SHALL provide a read-only explain_diagnostic capability that, given an SRS diagnostic code, returns that code's definition from the diagnostic registry (DR-PARSE-001 single source of truth): its code, title, severity, message template, source rule, since-version, and remediation guidance when present. For a registered code the lookup SHALL succeed and surface every definition field with its registry value (no undefined placeholder). For an unregistered code the capability SHALL return a structured not-found result with a non-zero/error outcome and a message that names the offending code, rather than throwing an unhandled error. The capability SHALL support a machine-readable JSON representation that exposes all definition fields as a single parseable object.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: For a known diagnostic code, the explain result surfaces the code, title, severity, message template, source rule, and since-version resolved from the diagnostic registry, and renders no 'undefined' placeholder.
- [ ] AC-2: The result includes the remediation text verbatim when the definition provides one, and omits remediation cleanly (no empty or 'undefined' remediation artifact) when the definition has none.
- [ ] AC-3: The `validate --explain <code>` entry point prints the same definition fields as the standalone explain, short-circuiting to a definition print with a success outcome for a known code instead of running a workspace validation.
- [ ] AC-4: An unregistered/unknown code produces a non-zero (error) outcome and a not-found style message that includes the offending code, not an unhandled thrown stack trace.
- [ ] AC-5: A JSON mode emits the definition as a single parseable, machine-readable object exposing code, title, severity, messageTemplate, sourceRule, since, and remediation fields matching the registry values.

### FR-MCP-052 — MCP work-mode tools get_work_mode and set_work_mode

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | tdd-mode |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The stdio MCP server SHALL expose the work-mode over MCP so agents can read and switch the mode (including turning TDD First mode on) without the CLI: `get_work_mode` (read-only) SHALL return the persisted mode and, for the task-scoped modes vibe/tdd, the Active Task, falling open to wait when docs/spec/steps/state.md is absent; `set_work_mode` (workspace mutation) SHALL accept mode sdd|vibe|wait|tdd with an optional activeTask (meaningful for vibe/tdd) and an optional dryRun, persisting through the FR-NODE-050/FR-NODE-071 work-mode model, and SHALL reject an out-of-enum mode with a structured INVALID_MODE error without writing. Both tools SHALL be registered consistently in the tool registry, the runtime tool schemas, and the read-only classification (get_work_mode read-only, set_work_mode not).

#### Rationale

TDD First mode shipped with a CLI-only toggle (IR-CLI-048/071; schemas.ts mode row carries no mcpName). Kiwi skills and MCP-first agents MUST use MCP tools when available, so turning tdd mode on must not require shelling out to the CLI.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: WHEN get_work_mode is called on a workspace whose state.md declares `Mode: tdd` and an Active Task THE tool SHALL return mode tdd with the activeTask, and WHEN state.md is absent THE tool SHALL return mode wait (fail-open).
- [x] AC-2: WHEN set_work_mode is called with {mode:"tdd", activeTask} THE mutation SHALL persist `Mode: tdd` and the Active Task line into state.md (a subsequent get_work_mode returns them), and dryRun SHALL write nothing.
- [x] AC-3: WHEN set_work_mode is called with an out-of-enum mode value THE tool SHALL fail with error code INVALID_MODE and state.md SHALL be unchanged.
- [x] AC-4: WHEN the MCP server registers its tools THE registry/toolSchemas/read-only classification SHALL stay set-consistent with both tools present, get_work_mode classified read-only (regression: existing tool set unchanged).

### FR-MCP-055 — Actionable recovery guidance for rejected per-call workspace root

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.4.2 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | feasibility:high, run-root, diagnostics |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/12.kiwi-wave-master-worktree-isolation-research.md |

#### Requirement

When the MCP server rejects a per-call `root` or `workspaceRoot` argument with `MCP_WORKSPACE_ROOT_UNSUPPORTED` and diagnostic `SRS-E075`, the structured recovery hint SHALL reference only actions the caller can actually perform. It SHALL NOT name a `restart_mcp_server` tool, which the server does not register and no caller can invoke. It SHALL instead state that the workspace root is resolved solely from the MCP server process working directory and that recovery requires starting the SpecKiwi MCP server — or the agent session that owns it — in the intended project directory. The fail-closed rejection behavior required by REL-MCP-004 AC-3 SHALL be unchanged.

#### Rationale

`src/mcp/adapter.ts:63` returns `recovery: { tool: "restart_mcp_server", ... }`, but that identifier appears exactly once in `src/` and is registered nowhere, so the hint directs an agent to a tool that does not exist; MCP process lifetime is owned by the harness, so the agent could not restart the server even if the tool existed. REL-MCP-004 AC-3 requires a structured diagnostic on rejection but says nothing about the hint being actionable, so this requirement extends that contract without weakening the rejection. No existing test asserts the recovery field, so the wrong string can regress undetected.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: If the rejection envelope carries a `recovery.tool`, its value is the name of a tool the server actually registers; `restart_mcp_server` is not registered and therefore does not appear.
- [x] AC-2: The recovery guidance states that the workspace root comes from the MCP server process working directory and that recovery means starting the server or owning session in the intended project directory.
- [x] AC-3: The rejection remains fail-closed and otherwise unchanged: error code, `SRS-E075` diagnostic, diagnostics summary, and `mcpWorkspace` identity fields keep their existing values and no mutation is performed.
- [x] AC-4: A regression test asserts the recovery hint contents so the guidance cannot silently regress to naming a non-existent tool.

### FR-MCP-056 — MCP exposes scope document creation and registration

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | v2.5.1 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | mcp, scope, scaffold |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/next/2026-07-30.numbering-and-2.5.0-residuals.md |

#### Requirement

The MCP server shall expose the scope scaffold and scope registration mutations as `scaffold_scope` and `register_scopes`, both workspace-kind, each accepting the apply and dry-run options its CLI counterpart accepts, so an agent restricted to MCP can create and register a scope document through a sanctioned mutation rather than by writing the file and the index rows by hand.

#### Rationale

The injected agent instructions require MCP tools to be used for safe SRS updates when they are available, but no MCP tool created a scope document. An MCP-only agent therefore had two options, both bad: hand-write the file and both index rows, or stop. Scope creation is precisely where hand-writing hurts, because the document number must be allocated and two index sections must agree — the reason `scaffold-scope` exists at all.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: An instantiated server registers `scaffold_scope` and `register_scopes`, both with mutation kind `workspace`.
- [x] AC-2: `scaffold_scope` over MCP allocates the next document number and creates the scope document, matching what the CLI produces for the same input.
- [x] AC-3: `register_scopes` over MCP inserts the planned Scope Map rows, matching its CLI counterpart.
- [x] AC-4: Both tools honour dry-run: the plan is returned and nothing is written.
- [x] AC-5: Both registry entries declare the MCP name, so the surface-parity contract covers them and the `commands` catalog stays a faithful projection.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/mcp/scope-tools.fr-mcp-056.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5 | 8 cases, all green. Audit 2026-07-30 rated this not promotable: AC-3 passed with the entire register_scopes core call replaced by ok:true, and AC-4 passed with scaffold_scope's only dry-run line deleted, because the test used dryRun without apply. AC-3 now asserts the Scope Map row and compares the MCP result to the CLI's index byte for byte; AC-4 now calls apply plus dryRun for both tools. Both re-probed red. |

### IR-MCP-003 — orchestrate MCP tool family mirroring the phase-1 CLI namespace

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | interface |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [orchestrator design](../research/kiwi-orchestrator/05.orchestrator-design.md) |

#### Requirement

The MCP server SHALL expose an `orchestrate_*` tool family mirroring the registered `speckiwi orchestrate` CLI namespace — `orchestrate_resume`, `orchestrate_preflight`, `orchestrate_route_probe`, `orchestrate_route_freeze`, `orchestrate_route_show`, `orchestrate_run_lock`, `orchestrate_run_abort`, `orchestrate_journal_append`, `orchestrate_card_write`, `orchestrate_freeze`, `orchestrate_readiness_check`, `orchestrate_schedule_plan`, `orchestrate_coupling_check`, `orchestrate_schedule_show`, `orchestrate_handoff_validate`, `orchestrate_round_record`, `orchestrate_issue_*`, `orchestrate_wave_close`, `orchestrate_duplication_plan`, `orchestrate_validate` and `orchestrate_auto_gate` for the phase-1 rows, together with one tool for each phase-2 row a later target has landed — registered through the existing `src/mcp/schemas.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts` and `src/mcp/tools/{read,mutation}-tools.ts` pattern rather than a parallel registration path, with every mutation tool returning the standard mutation envelope and supporting a dry-run input. Every phase-1 CLI row SHALL have an MCP counterpart, and no CLI row still listed as deferred SHALL have one — so no `orchestrate_lane_audit`, `orchestrate_lane_harvest`, `orchestrate_lane_release` or `orchestrate_lane_status` tool is registered.

#### Rationale

05 §10.6's MCP paragraph mirrors the CLI namespace through the existing registration pattern and defers the four lane verbs with the CLI rows that take a lane workspace or a lane manifest as their subject. §10.6's exit-code and envelope paragraph states that every mutation verb returns the standard `MutationEnvelope` and honours `--dry-run` so a gate refusal is inspectable before it is consequential, and that a dry run of a call that would be refused refuses identically.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Listing the server's tools returns every `orchestrate_*` tool named in the requirement.
- [x] AC-2: No tool named `orchestrate_lane_audit`, `orchestrate_lane_harvest`, `orchestrate_lane_release` or `orchestrate_lane_status` is registered, and the registered `orchestrate_*` set is exactly the named phase-1 family plus the tools later targets have landed.
- [x] AC-3: Every registered `orchestrate_*` tool resolves to a registered CLI verb row, every phase-1 CLI row has an MCP counterpart, and no CLI row still listed as deferred has one — asserted in both directions as a mapping test across the two surfaces.
- [x] AC-4: Every `orchestrate_*` mutation tool returns the standard mutation envelope carrying `applied`, and a refused call returns `applied: false` with a `gate` naming the refusal.
- [x] AC-5: A dry-run call of a mutation tool leaves its target file byte-identical, and a dry-run call of one that would be refused returns the refusal envelope rather than a success envelope.
- [x] AC-6: The tools are registered through the existing `schemas.ts` / `server.ts` / `{read,mutation}-tools.ts` files; no second registration path is introduced.

AC-1 asserts the live server registers each of the 25 named phase-1 orchestrate_* tools; the 25 is a fact about the PHASE-1 family and deliberately does not grow when phase 2 lands a row — tools from later targets are listed separately in REGISTERED_BEYOND_PHASE1. AC-2 asserts none of orchestrate_lane_{audit,harvest,release,status} is registered and that the registered orchestrate_* set equals the named family plus REGISTERED_BEYOND_PHASE1 exactly, so a tool appearing from nowhere still fails. AC-3 asserts every ORCHESTRATE_TOOL_BINDINGS entry names a registered CLI leaf whose row is in ORCHESTRATE_REGISTERED_VERB_ROWS, and — added 2026-08-10 — asserts the mirror in BOTH directions: no deferred row is mirrored, AND no phase-1 row is left unmirrored. The second direction was previously carried by nothing, which left the Requirement's own claim to mirror the CLI namespace falsifiable with every test green; that is the class of defect measurement alone cannot catch. ### IR-MCP-004 — Every mirrored orchestrate CLI leaf's required options are declared on its MCP binding

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | interface |
| Target | 2.6.0-phase1-kiwi-orchestrator |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

Every option a mirrored `orchestrate` CLI leaf declares as required SHALL appear in that leaf's `ORCHESTRATE_TOOL_BINDINGS` entry, so that every registered `orchestrate_*` tool is invocable through MCP. A binding that omits such an option SHALL be reported by an automated check naming both the tool and the flag.

#### Rationale

`IR-MCP-003` AC-6 asserts the binding-to-schema direction: every binding option is a field of the tool's input schema. Nothing asserts the converse, and the converse is what makes a tool callable. `orchestrateArgv` emits a flag only for an option the binding declares, so a required CLI option missing from the binding can never be passed, and commander then refuses the call — `argv.push("--json")` puts the run in json mode, so the missing mandatory option becomes exit 2 rather than a usage message. The input schema is derived from the same binding, so a caller cannot supply the value deliberately either. Measured: `orchestrate round record` gained a required `--proof` and its binding did not, which left `orchestrate_round_record` uncallable in every case from the moment it shipped, with no test able to notice. The sibling `orchestrate_issue_resolve` declares the same option correctly, so the omission is a gap in the guard rather than in the pattern.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: `orchestrate_round_record`'s binding declares `--proof` as a required option, and `orchestrateArgv` given an input carrying `proof` emits `--proof` in the argv it builds.
- [x] AC-2: For every entry in `ORCHESTRATE_TOOL_BINDINGS`, every option the named CLI leaf declares with `requiredOption` appears among that entry's options under the same flag; when one does not, the failure names the tool and the flag rather than only a count.
- [x] AC-3: The census is non-vacuous in both directions: it resolves every binding to a CLI leaf that really exists, and at least one leaf really declares a required option, so neither an empty binding list nor a traversal that finds no required options anywhere can pass it.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/mcp/orchestrate-binding-completeness.ir-mcp-004.test.ts | AC-1, AC-2, AC-3 | 2 passed / 0 failed, 2026-08-02. Red first: 2 failed / 0 passed before the binding was touched. AC-1 failed with the binding must declare --proof, expected undefined to be defined. AC-2 failed naming exactly one offender repo-wide, orchestrate_round_record omits --proof, which is the whole population of the defect and confirms the sibling bindings were already correct. The census walks the live commander tree built by buildCommand plus registerOrchestrateCommands and reads each leaf's mandatory options, so it measures the shipped CLI rather than a restatement of it. AC-3 was green from the first run and is evidenced by mutation: renaming one binding path to a leaf that does not exist takes the leaf-resolution guard red and names the tool, expected orchestrate_round_record to deeply equal empty; source restored and re-verified. The second floor is a count over declared required options, which fails on an empty traversal, so a census that found nothing could not read as compliance. Regression: 28 files / 122 passed across test/mcp, and both typecheck projects clean. |

### FR-MCP-057 — MCP workflow record reclassification

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.7.0-verification-integrity |
| Status | planned |
| Priority | critical |
| Tags | mcp, workflow, record-reclassification, provenance, feasibility:high |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | Automated MCP schema and handler tests plus field-for-field CLI/MCP parity and workflow reader tests. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The SpecKiwi MCP server shall register a typed workflow_record_reclassification tool that delegates preview and apply operations to the NODE core service, exposes the complete guarded mutation result, and has field-for-field behavioral parity with the CLI reclassify-record command.

#### Rationale

Project agents require an official MCP recovery path that preserves the same provenance and fail-closed boundaries as core and CLI. A typed schema prevents arbitrary malformed repair events and keeps mutation behavior in one core implementation.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: The MCP schema exposes runId, optional path, recordType as the closed enum pipeline or worklog, one-based line, zero-based byteOffset, rawSha256, eventKey, targetRunId, preimagePrefixSha256, expectedSha256, owner, non-empty reason, optional taskId, optional reqId, optional idempotencyKey, optional repairToken, and dryRun, and exposes no arbitrary event field; dryRun false requires repairToken and expectedSha256.
- [ ] AC-2: When dryRun is true, the tool creates no journal, lock, recovery guard, quarantine, sentinel, or other artifact and returns written false plus the core repairToken, typed pendingRepair, predicted overlay operation, diagnostic delta, target identity, resolved artifact kind/path, and journal identity.
- [ ] AC-3: When dryRun is false, the tool forwards every input field unchanged to workflow_record_reclassification; core consumes only the token-bound typed pendingRepair and the adapter returns the shared mutation envelope without removing operations, journal metadata, artifact and target metadata, stale guards, pendingRepair, diagnostics, or diagnosticsSummary.
- [ ] AC-4: The MCP adapter preserves core ordering in which the opaque token-bound canonical request is verified before replay and a valid exact replay returns a successful no-write result before stale-preimage rejection; path-kind mismatch, unknown or legacy kind, stale input, ambiguity, conflict, unrelated diagnostic, invalid or missing token, forged canonical-looking timestamp, or identity mismatch returns a structured no-write failure. Post-append confirmation and lock-cleanup failures preserve the core completedOperations, pendingOperations, typed pendingRepair kind and fields, diagnostics, and written false result. Identical and conflicting concurrent requests expose exactly the core outcomes specified by FR-NODE-177 AC-7.
- [ ] AC-5: Every MCP reader derived from the shared JSONL projection preserves it, including workflow_next_plan_task, workflow_doctor, workflow_diff, workflow_schema_check, workflow_pipeline_status, workflow_pipeline_tail, workflow_pipeline_next, workflow_pipeline_compact, workflow_worklog_tail, workflow_session_status, workflow_resume_hint, and get_next_work_order; their effective results include the same audit_note/meta annotation, targeted SRS-W054 removal, raw-audit entries where returned, latest-state exclusion, and correction-graph exclusion as core and CLI readers.
- [ ] AC-6: MCP and CLI parity tests submit the same artifact-free dry-run, typed-pendingRepair, token-before-replay, apply, replay, stale, malformed, forged-timestamp, path-kind, ambiguous, conflicting, concurrent-identical, concurrent-conflicting, unrelated-diagnostic, post-append confirmation-failure, lock-cleanup-failure, generic-repair rejection, and named and derived reader fixtures, including whitespace-only generic correction rejection, and assert equal effective results, durable provenance, completed and pending operations, pendingRepair kind and fields, and diagnostic sets.

### REL-MCP-005 — MCP workspace root identity and safety (host-fixed SRS, per-call run state)

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | reliability |
| Target | 2.11.0-mcp-worktree-root |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | 2.11.0, mcp, workspace, root, safety, worktree, user-directive |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test, inspection |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/18.mcp-per-call-worktree-root-research.md |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP SHALL resolve its startup root only from the server process working directory and expose no root parameter on any startup surface, and SHALL accept an optional per-call absolute `workspaceRoot` only on a tool that declares a workspace scope, which no tool that writes under `docs/spec/` or allocates a Requirement ID may declare, so the argument is refused fail-closed on every such tool. An accepted `workspaceRoot` SHALL be an existing git top level that shares the git common directory of the startup root, SHALL be validated before any tool handler runs, SHALL refuse a path argument whose destination falls under `docs/spec/` unless the tool declares that it takes no caller-supplied path, and SHALL be reported back through the response envelope.

#### Rationale

-
- [2026-08-16] 사용자 지시(2026-08-16)로 per-call 금지를 철회한다. REL-MCP-004 는 성격이 다른 두 금지를 한 요구에 묶었고, 그것이 이 마찰의 원인이다. 기동 인자 금지(그 AC-4, 여기 AC-4 로 계승)의 근거는 잘못된 root 로 서버가 떠서 조용히 오염되는 경로를 막는 것이다. 그 근거는 per-call 인자(그 AC-3, 여기서 철회)에 적용되지 않는다 — per-call 은 호출마다 의도가 명시되고 envelope 의 rootSource 로 되비쳐지므로 오히려 덜 조용하다.
- [2026-08-16] 저장소는 이미 이 논거를 받아들여 요구로 승격했다. IR-CLI-082 AC-6 은 orchestrate preflight 가 두 root 를 모두 인자로 받게 하고, 그 Rationale 은 MCP 서버가 workspaceRoot 를 자기 프로세스 cwd 로 고정하므로 도구 내부에서 수행하는 비교는 공허하다고 적는다. 따라서 이 요구는 새 원칙의 도입이 아니라 이미 채택된 원칙을 나머지 도구로 넓히는 것이다.
- [2026-08-16] 다중 root 는 다섯 번 거절됐다(2026-05-26 / 07-06 / 07-27 / 07-31 / 08-07). 거절 사유의 핵심은 요구 ID 가 두 곳에서 발급돼 병합 시 SRS-E002 중복이 나고 인덱스 롤업이 갈라지는 것이었다. 여기서는 SRS 를 읽거나 변이하는 도구가 인자를 얻지 못하므로 그 사유가 구조적으로 발생할 수 없다 — 거절을 우회하는 것이 아니라, 거절 사유가 닿지 않는 부분집합만 연다.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: MCP exposes a workspace information tool and every read/mutation result envelope carries workspaceRoot, rootSource, index path, active target, package version and diagnosticsSummary.
- [x] AC-2: rootSource reports exactly one of server-cwd-discovery, auto-init, or per-call-workspace-root, and reports per-call-workspace-root precisely when the call supplied a workspaceRoot that passed every gate — so a caller can always tell which root answered it.
- [x] AC-3: A tool that writes under docs/spec or allocates a Requirement ID refuses a per-call root or workspaceRoot argument fail-closed with a structured diagnostic, and that includes a tool which only computes the next free ID and hands it back without writing it. The refusal is the registration default: acceptance is carried by a declared workspace scope, and the declaration is read as membership of a closed set of scopes, each naming the subject it admits rather than borrowing another family's name. A tool that declares none inherits the refusal, and so does one whose declared scope is misspelt, so a newly added SRS mutation tool is refused without being listed anywhere. The boundary is authorship, not reading — a tool that only reads what a root holds is not caught by this criterion, which is why AC-6 guards destinations separately.
- [x] AC-4: startMcpServer and McpServerOptions expose no root parameter, and MCP startup root resolution has only the cwd-upward-search and auto-init paths.
- [x] AC-5: An accepted workspaceRoot is absolute, is an existing directory, is a git top level rather than a subdirectory of one, and resolves to the same git common directory as the startup root; each failure is refused with its own diagnostic code before any handler runs, so a workspaceRoot naming a path that does not exist is refused rather than created.
- [x] AC-6: Refusal is by destination as well as by tool identity: a tool that accepts workspaceRoot refuses a path argument resolving under docs/spec of the accepted root, with a workspace-root-forbidden-for-srs diagnostic. Which of a tool's arguments are paths is declared and never inferred: a tool that declares nothing has every one of its arguments treated as a path, so a newly added tool is guarded without being listed anywhere, and only a tool declaring that it takes no caller-supplied path at all is scanned no further — which is what lets an SRS query filter on a docs/spec reference. This is asserted on the `path` argument the checkbox-mutation tools take straight from the caller (the schema names it `path`, and no argument named planPath exists), whose value reaches resolveInsideRoot with containment as its only check — classifying by tool family alone would leave a worktree's SRS writable outside every SRS gate — and on a workflow_* read whose path lands under docs/spec, so the exemption does not reach a family that declares nothing.
- [x] AC-7: A repository that is not the startup root's own is neither read nor mutated, proven twice: once where the supplied workspaceRoot is a separate repository rather than a worktree of the startup root, and once for the case inherited from the superseded requirement, where no per-call argument is given and the caller's intended workspace differs from the resolved root identity.
- [x] AC-8: MCP documentation and managed agent instructions state which tool families accept workspaceRoot and require callers to confirm workspace identity from the envelope before target-scoped reads or mutations; the kiwi-orchestrator and kiwi-wave-master skills, which today teach only the CLI --mcp-root/--git-root pattern, name the MCP argument as well.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/mcp/workspace-root-gate.rel-mcp-005.test.ts; test/mcp/workspace-root-docs.rel-mcp-005.test.ts; test/mcp/root-source-auto-init.rel-mcp-005.test.ts | AC-1; AC-2; AC-4; AC-5; AC-6; AC-7; AC-8 | TDD red-to-green: the gate file opened 10 failed / 4 passed, and two criteria that passed trivially (AC-5 handler-ordering, and IR-MCP-005 AC-3's sibling) were STRENGTHENED before implementation and re-confirmed red, taking it to 11 red / 3 pass. Load-bearing proved by mutation, not by passing: nine deliberate breaks each turned a test red - foreign-repo compare always-true (AC-7 red), existence check skipped (AC-5 red), subdirectory of a git top level accepted (AC-2 and AC-5 red), srsDestination forced null (AC-6 red), the SRS-tool refusal made permissive (AC-3 red, an SRS tool mutated across the worktree boundary), rootSource pinned to server-cwd-discovery (4 red), replay_apply switched to acceptance, the preflight role options removed, and validation moved after the handler (5 red, with writes reaching paths that should have been refused, one under docs/spec). AC-7 needed two proofs and initially had one and a half: the inherited no-argument case asserted only envelope identity and a string absence, neither of the verbs the criterion names. It was rewritten to attempt four reads and four writes against a sibling repository seeded with markers found nowhere else, comparing a relativePath-to-sha256 digest of the whole sibling before and after; it passed on first run, so it was mutation-proved separately by disabling resolveInsideRoot's containment guard, where the broken build returned the sibling's plan body, its run id and a ../-escaping relativePath, and a throwaway probe showed the write half accepted an append into the sibling. RETRACTED 2026-09-04, and the sentence it replaces is not restored because it was wrong twice over. It read: an independent adversarial pass drove all 100 registered tools against a real linked worktree, a foreign repository and a nonexistent path, observing 49 refused (47 SRS-facing plus orchestrate_preflight and orchestrate_replay_apply), 51 accepted and honoured, zero silently ignored. That pass ran through the in-process adapter handle, which sits downstream of the SDK's argument parsing, so it measured the gate rather than the protocol surface this requirement is about; re-measured over a real client-to-server roundtrip on the same commit, the protocol surface refused 1, accepted 51 and silently ignored 48. Its composition figure is off by one as well: the 49 are 46 SRS-facing tools plus preview_legacy_workflow_migration plus the two orchestrate rows, not 47 plus two. Both corrections are FR-MCP-063's measurement and VE-2 carries them; AC-3 is no longer covered by this row for that reason. What that pass did establish and what this row still stands on: update_status and add_requirement refused with both checkouts byte-identical by sha256; 15 spellings of a docs/spec destination all refused including backslash, DOCS/SPEC, kiwi/../docs/spec and a trailing dot; a nonexistent workspaceRoot refused without the directory being created; and a tool registered with no metadata at all was refused, proving the registration default is fail-closed. Suite 399 files / 5670 passed / 4 skipped / 1 failed, the single failure being the pre-existing load-dependent srs-lock-status-cache flake, which this change does not touch, which imports none of the changed modules, and which passed 3/3 run alone. typecheck, tsconfig.test typecheck, lint --max-warnings=0 and build all clean. |
| VE-2 | test | test/mcp/protocol-path-argument.fr-mcp-063.test.ts | AC-3 | Retraction and correction of VE-1's adversarial census. VE-1 says it drove all 100 registered tools and observed "49 refused / 51 accepted / zero silently ignored"; that pass ran through the in-process adapter handle, which sits downstream of the SDK's argument parsing. Re-measured on 1340baca over a real client-to-server roundtrip: 1 refused / 51 accepted / 48 silently ignored for workspaceRoot, and 0 refused / 100 silently ignored for root, because a zod object deletes keys its shape does not declare and no tool declares either key as an argument. The single observable refusal was preview_legacy_workflow_migration - and only because its schema advertised a workspaceRoot the gate refuses, so the one place schema and gate disagreed is also what hid the other 48. VE-1's composition figure is off by one for the same reason: the 49 are 46 SRS-facing tools plus that tool plus orchestrate_preflight and orchestrate_replay_apply, not "47 SRS-facing" plus the two. AC-3's own wording is not withdrawn - it is true at both layers once the seam carries the keys - but the verification behind it did not exercise the surface the requirement is about, which is why Status returned to implemented. FR-MCP-063 owns that reachability and drives the protocol; its evidence records that restoring the old wiring leaves this requirement's own gate suite green at 14 passed while the protocol suite reddens. |
| VE-3 | test | test/mcp/srs-read-workspace-root.fr-mcp-064.test.ts | AC-3; AC-6 | The two criteria this requirement's 2026-09-04 revision widened are covered here rather than by the three files above, which predate the revision. Written for FR-MCP-064 and driven over a real MCP client-to-server roundtrip. AC-3: a census walks every registered tool with a root that fails the first identity check, so no handler runs, and asserts each one lands in the accepting or the refusing set with no third answer, which is the registration default deciding. It then holds diagnose_requirement_id_collisions and plan_requirement_id_collision_repair inside the refusing set by name, which is the clause about a tool that only computes the next free ID and hands it back without writing it - both reach allocateNextId, one for candidateReplacementIds and one for replacementId - and it holds mcp_workspace_info there over the protocol as well, answering MCP_WORKSPACE_ROOT_UNSUPPORTED with the startup root in the envelope. AC-6: exactly the thirteen SRS query tools carry a callerPathKeys declaration and each declares it empty, compared for equality in both directions so a fourteenth declaration reddens; a list_requirements call whose relatedDoc is docs/spec/10.product-architecture.srs.md answers instead of being refused, which is the docs/spec filter the declaration exists to permit; and a workflow_plan_status call whose path lands under docs/spec of the same accepted worktree is still refused with workspace-root-forbidden-for-srs, so the exemption does not reach a family that declares nothing. What this row does not carry: AC-3's misspelt-scope case, which lives in the gate suite VE-1 names and which the mutation record shows is the one that separates a closed-set membership test from a presence test; and the refusal reason of the write family, which this file only counts as a census denominator. |

- [2026-08-16] 정정 — 위 첫 노트의 "B군은 read-tools.ts:51 projectRoot 하나" 는 틀렸다. 주입 지점은 둘이다: projectRoot(deps)(read-tools.ts:51)가 workflow read 17개, root(deps, input)(mutation-tools.ts:33)가 workflow mutation 9개. 17+9=26 으로 런타임 수와 맞는다. 9번째(workflow_record_reclassification, mutation-tools.ts:472)는 이름이 다음 줄이라 grep 패턴에서 빠진다. 독립 검증자가 잡았고 서버를 띄워 도구 이름을 세어 확정했다.
- [2026-08-16] 그 둘째 지점은 함정이다. root(deps, input) 의 호출부는 38곳이고 workflow 는 9개뿐이며, 나머지에 update_status(:205)·supersede_requirement(:607) 등 SRS 변이 도구가 들어 있다. 시그니처가 이미 input 을 받고 void input; 으로 버리므로 "여기서 input.workspaceRoot 를 존중시키면 된다"가 가장 자연스러워 보이지만, 그렇게 하면 A군 SRS 변이가 전부 같이 열려 AC-3 을 정면으로 어긴다. 공유 헬퍼의 의미를 바꾸지 말고, 유효 root 는 분류 게이트가 정해 넘겨라.
- [2026-08-31] FR-NODE-204 가 run-lock.ts 에 RunLock.holder 필드와 runLockHolder 투영과 doc 주석을 더하면서, 위 노트가 못박은 resolveGitCommonDir(run-lock.ts:81) 이 89 로 밀렸다. 그 함수와 그것이 처리하는 함정 둘은 그대로이고 줄 번호만 낡았다. verified 요구라 표 편집이 MUTATION_DENIED 로 거절되므로 정정을 여기에 남긴다.
- [2026-09-04] 어댑터 핸들만 부르는 테스트는 이 요구가 약속한 표면을 잴 수 없다. SDK 가 도구의 선언 스키마로 인자를 먼저 파싱하고 그 뒤에 게이트 래퍼가 돌기 때문에, 스키마에 없는 키는 게이트가 보기 전에 지워진다. 실측: 결함을 되돌려도 게이트 스위트(test/mcp/workspace-root-gate.rel-mcp-005.test.ts)는 14 통과로 초록을 유지한다. 프로토콜 왕복은 FR-MCP-063 이 맡는다.
- [2026-09-04] VE-1 의 Covers 를 `all` 에서 AC-3 을 뺀 일곱으로 좁혔다. 그 행이 기록한 "49 refused / zero silently ignored" 와 "47 SRS-facing" 은 어댑터 층 측정치이고 AC-3 이 바로 그 층 착오의 자리이므로, AC-3 의 증거는 프로토콜 표면을 실제로 몬 VE-2 가 진다. 나머지 일곱은 그 적대적 검사가 여전히 덮는다. 재승격 전에 그 일곱도 같은 층에서 재는지 판단이 필요하다 — 이 작업은 AC-3 의 도달 가능성만 쟀다.

### FR-MCP-058 — workflow_* tools resolve their paths against a per-call workspace root

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.11.0-mcp-worktree-root |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

Every tool in the `workflow_*` family SHALL accept an optional absolute `workspaceRoot` and, when one is supplied and accepted, SHALL resolve every path it reads or writes against that root instead of the MCP startup root, so a session whose MCP server is fixed to the host checkout can address the plans, session state, pipeline and worklog that live in a worktree.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Each workflow_* tool accepts workspaceRoot, and omitting it reproduces today's behaviour byte for byte — the argument is additive, so an existing caller is unaffected.
- [x] AC-2: With workspaceRoot supplied, a relative `path` argument resolves against that root: a plan present only in the worktree is found, and the same call without workspaceRoot does not find it.
- [x] AC-3: An absolute `path` argument pointing inside the supplied workspaceRoot is accepted, and one pointing outside it is refused. The containment check is reused rather than reimplemented, but it is not sufficient on its own: a path that stays inside the root and still lands under docs/spec is refused per REL-MCP-005 AC-6, because containment was written to stop traversal and never to draw a root boundary.
- [x] AC-4: A write-capable workflow_* tool given a workspaceRoot writes inside that root and leaves the startup root untouched, proven by asserting the startup root's tree is unchanged after the call — asserted on a tool reached through mutation-tools.ts, not only on a read tool, because the two families take their root from different helpers.
- [x] AC-5: The response envelope of a call carrying an accepted workspaceRoot reports that root and a rootSource of per-call-workspace-root, so the caller can tell the answer did not come from the startup root.
- [x] AC-6: Pointing workflow_task_check, workflow_task_uncheck or workflow_checklist_set at an SRS document under the accepted workspaceRoot is refused rather than toggling an acceptance-criteria checkbox. The case is named because those tools take planPath straight from the caller and SRS files carry the same `- [ ] ` shape a plan does, so nothing about the payload would tell them apart.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/mcp/workflow-workspace-root.fr-mcp-058.test.ts; test/mcp/workspace-root-gate.rel-mcp-005.test.ts | all | Stated honestly rather than flattered: only 3 of the 6 criteria opened red in their own file, because the workflow_* threading had already landed under REL-MCP-005's red tests, which use workflow_workspace_info and workflow_task_check as their vehicles. The three that were green on first run are not therefore unasserted — mutation proved them load-bearing: forcing srsDestination to null turned AC-3 and AC-6 red, and pinning rootSource to server-cwd-discovery turned AC-5 red. AC-4 is asserted on the mutation path the family actually splits over, not only the read path: the case calls workflow_pipeline_emit, registered in mutation-tools.ts through the root(deps, input) helper rather than read-tools.ts's projectRoot(deps), and asserts the lane's kiwi/pipeline.jsonl gained the write while a tree digest of the host root is byte-for-byte unchanged. That split is the defect this requirement's first draft missed — an independent audit found the Implementation Notes naming one injection point when there are two, and the 9 write-capable tools reached only by the second would have been left unable to honour the argument. AC-6 names the checkbox tools specifically: an adversarial pass drove 15 spellings of a docs/spec destination through workflow_task_check — forward slash, backslash, DOCS/SPEC under Windows case folding, ./docs/spec, kiwi/../docs/spec, docs//spec, docs/./spec, docs/spec/../spec, trailing space, trailing dot, and both absolute forms — and every one was refused with workspace-root-forbidden-for-srs, with zero bypasses. AC-1's additivity was checked by the same adversarial pass finding zero silently-ignored tools across all 100: no tool declares the argument and then quietly answers from the startup root. |

### FR-MCP-059 — orchestrate_* tools carry a per-call workspace root into the CLI they mirror

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 2.11.0-mcp-worktree-root |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

Every tool in the `orchestrate_*` family except `orchestrate_replay_apply` and `orchestrate_preflight` SHALL accept an optional absolute `workspaceRoot` and, when one is supplied and accepted, SHALL run the CLI leaf it mirrors against that root rather than the MCP startup root. `orchestrate_replay_apply` SHALL refuse it, because replaying a deferred SRS mutation anywhere but the host root is what the deferral exists to prevent. `orchestrate_preflight` SHALL NOT gain it, because it already takes both roots it judges as required arguments.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: An accepted workspaceRoot reaches the mirrored CLI leaf as its root, so the tool reads and writes the same files the same command would from a shell whose working directory is that root — asserted on the argv the binding produces, not only on the outcome.
- [x] AC-2: Omitting workspaceRoot produces the argv it produces today, so the argument is additive.
- [x] AC-3: orchestrate_replay_apply refuses a workspaceRoot fail-closed with a diagnostic naming the host-root rule, and the refusal is asserted by a test that would fail if the tool merely ignored the argument.
- [x] AC-4: orchestrate_preflight gains no workspaceRoot: it already takes --mcp-root and --git-root as required arguments, and a test asserts its schema declares neither a root nor a workspaceRoot field so a third way to name the subject cannot appear.
- [x] AC-5: A journal or card path given alongside a workspaceRoot resolves against that root, and the run it appends to is the worktree's run — the startup root's journal is unchanged, asserted by reading it after the call.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/mcp/orchestrate-workspace-root-argv.fr-mcp-059.test.ts; test/mcp/orchestrate-workspace-root.fr-mcp-059.test.ts | all | TDD, and the discipline is worth recording because it nearly lapsed: the orchestrate_* implementation was begun without its own failing test, the lapse was caught, and the whole of it — the callOrchestrateTool context parameter, the metadata table and the schema wiring — was REVERTED and rewritten test-first, giving 7 red across AC-1, AC-3, AC-5 and the companion case that every non-excluded row declares and forwards the field. AC-1 is asserted on the argv the binding produces rather than only on the outcome, so a tool that reached the right files by some other route would not satisfy it. AC-3 was mutation-proved: switching orchestrate_replay_apply from refusal to worktree-local acceptance turned it red, so the criterion is not satisfied by a tool that merely ignores the argument. AC-4's exclusion of orchestrate_preflight is asserted negatively — its schema must declare neither a root nor a workspaceRoot field — which is what stops a third way of naming the subject appearing beside the --mcp-root and --git-root it already requires. The independent adversarial pass confirms the split at runtime: of the 27 orchestrate_* tools, 25 accept and honour the argument, and orchestrate_preflight and orchestrate_replay_apply are refused, the latter with workspace-root-forbidden-for-replay-apply. AC-2's additivity claim is supported by that pass finding zero silently-ignored tools. One weakness stated rather than hidden: AC-2 has no paired negative case in its own file, and rests on the shared gate's refusals being exercised generically elsewhere. |

### IR-MCP-005 — orchestrate_preflight lets a lane declare that it is a lane

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | interface |
| Target | 2.11.0-mcp-worktree-root |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | - |

#### Requirement

The `orchestrate_preflight` MCP binding SHALL expose the role declaration its CLI leaf accepts — `--role`, `--lane-id` and `--lane-plan` — so a run in a linked worktree can claim the lane role over MCP. Without them the binding can only ever produce the default host role, and the role-aware gate refuses a linked worktree claiming to be a host, which makes the topology gate unreachable from the surface a worktree session actually uses.

#### Rationale

-

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The orchestrate_preflight tool schema declares role, laneId and lanePlan, and the argv the binding produces carries the flags the CLI leaf defines for them.
- [x] AC-2: A call declaring role lane against a linked worktree passes the gate where the same call without the role declaration is refused. Both host-role refusals are asserted separately, because they are reached by different pairs: mcpRoot and gitRoot both pointing at the worktree reaches host-root-is-a-linked-worktree, while mcpRoot at the host and gitRoot at the lane is refused earlier by the legacy equality gate, which runs first for role host and whose refusal wins. Naming only one of the two would let the other regress unseen.
- [x] AC-3: A call declaring role lane without a lane id, or with a lane id absent from the frozen lane plan, is refused: exposing the declaration does not weaken IR-CLI-093's rule that the role is declared and then checked, never inferred.
- [x] AC-4: Every option the preflight CLI leaf defines is present in its MCP binding, asserted by deriving the expected set from the command definition rather than from a list written in the test — so a future option added to the leaf and forgotten in the binding fails this criterion instead of going unnoticed.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | test/mcp/preflight-role-binding.ir-mcp-005.test.ts | all | TDD red-to-green: AC-1, AC-2 and AC-4 opened red, and AC-3 passed trivially on first run, so it was strengthened before implementation and re-confirmed red. Mutation-proved: removing role, laneId and lanePlan from the ORCHESTRATE_TOOL_BINDINGS row turned all four cases red together, which is the right shape — the requirement exists because their absence made the whole role-aware gate unreachable from MCP, not because any one case is delicate. AC-2 was corrected during implementation because the criterion as first authored was WRONG: it named host-root-is-a-linked-worktree for a call with mcpRoot at the host and gitRoot at the lane, and that pair cannot produce it, because for role host the legacy equality gate at orchestrate.ts:548 runs first and its refusal wins. The two host-role refusals are reached by different pairs and are now asserted separately, so neither can regress behind the other. AC-4 derives its expected option set from the CLI leaf's own command definition rather than from a list written in the test, so an option added to the leaf and forgotten in the binding fails this criterion instead of going unnoticed — which is exactly how the gap this requirement closes went unnoticed until now. Final: 14/14 in the gate file, 4/4 here, typecheck and lint clean. |

### FR-MCP-060 — Every MCP tool registration carries a description projected from the ToolSpec registry

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 3.1.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | [plan item 14](../plan/3.0/14.mcp-tool-description.md), [audit 28 §4.4](../research/28.skills-mcp-end-to-end-audit.md) |

#### Requirement

When the MCP server registers its tool surface, it SHALL hand the SDK a description projected from the `ToolSpec` registry for every tool it registers, so that a `tools/list` response carries a non-empty description for all 100 tools; the registry SHALL take the MCP tool name and its description as one declaration, so a tool named without a description does not compile; and the server SHALL refuse to register a tool name the registry does not describe rather than register it without a description.

#### Rationale

`docs/research/28.skills-mcp-end-to-end-audit.md` §4.4 measured a real `tools/list` response: description non-empty for 0 of 100 tools, `title` equal to `name` for 100 of 100. An agent choosing among `list_requirements` and `search_requirements`, `workflow_pipeline_status` and `workflow_pipeline_tail`, or `get_active_target` and `summarize_target` gets only the name and the argument list, so the choosing criterion migrated into skill prose that loads on every skill invocation whether or not the tool is called. Putting the semantics on the tool moves that cost to once per session and is the precondition the plan's item 12 (skill body reduction) depends on. The payload grows; the plan's §6 states that trade explicitly and forbids treating this as a byte-saving exercise.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Every `ToolSpec` that declares an `mcpName` also declares a `description` of at least 60 characters, across all 100 MCP-exposed specs, and the tool name and its description arrive as one builder argument so that naming a tool without describing it is a compile error rather than a runtime omission.
- [x] AC-2: A `tools/list` round trip over the MCP protocol, against the server this repository registers, returns 100 tools, each carrying a non-empty `description` byte-identical to the one the registry holds for that name, and none of them carrying a `title` — the field this change stopped sending, because the protocol displays `name` when `title` is absent and a `title` equal to `name` repeats a value the client already holds.
- [x] AC-3: Over the derived confusable-pair set — two tool names are confusable when they share every name token but the last, or share their final token normalised for a trailing `s` — every pair satisfies three conditions: the two descriptions are different strings, each description carries at least one content word (four letters or more, outside a stopword list, and not a token of either tool's own name) the other does not, and that word is absent from the other description. The derived set holds at least 119 pairs over at least 81 distinct tools and contains (`list_requirements`, `search_requirements`), (`workflow_pipeline_status`, `workflow_pipeline_tail`) and (`get_active_target`, `summarize_target`).
- [x] AC-4: Every description discloses what the tool changes: all 52 tools `isReadOnlyTool` rejects carry the marker `Writes `, all 48 it accepts carry the marker `Read-only.`, and no description carries both markers.
- [x] AC-5: The registration payload is projected from the registry rather than restated beside it — the server hands the SDK the description the registry holds — and a registered tool name the registry does not describe fails registration with an error naming that tool instead of registering it without a description.
- [x] AC-6: Each judge the suite runs answers a known question in both directions, over samples whose verdict is fixed by construction rather than read off the shipped surface. The pair judge refuses a pair whose two descriptions are identical, refuses a pair whose first description contributes no content word the second lacks, and passes a pair of genuinely distinct descriptions. The byte comparison AC-2 runs refuses a listing carrying the registry's text with a sentence written beside it, refuses a listing that arrived with no description at all, and passes a listing projected from the registry byte for byte. Both judges take the description source as a parameter so that those samples can reach them, which is what the samples are for: rewritten to hold that source against itself, either judge reports a clean surface over one that has entirely drifted, and no assertion that counts violations can tell those two readings apart. Measured for the second of them before this criterion covered it: with the comparison holding the registry against itself, all 100 listed descriptions had drifted from the registry and the file stayed green at sixteen tests.
- [x] AC-7: Every value that decides how far this suite reaches is read from this requirement rather than held as a literal in the suite's own source, so narrowing a check means editing the criterion that check answers. Four rosters are anchored that way: the criteria this file must declare a block for are the criteria listed here, compared in both directions so that a block answering a criterion this requirement does not declare fails as well as a criterion left without a block; the tools whose closed lists AC-8 compares are the tools AC-8 names; the confusable pairs the derivation has to survive are the pairs AC-3 names; and the floors are these — `tools 100`, `specs 149`, `pairs 119`, `pairedTools 81`, `readOnly 48`, `mutation 52`, `stopwords 99`, `criteria 9`, `enumRows 5`, `namedPairs 3` — each asserted against a value the suite derives, from the shipped registry for the first seven and from this requirement for the last three, so that a registry reduced to a stub fails instead of reporting zero violations over the survivors and a roster read out of this requirement and then filtered on the way in fails on its count rather than on the entry it dropped. The suite further asserts that it reads every floor named here, at the value named here, and that no floor here is zero, so that a floor cannot be declared and then left unasserted, cannot be read through an accessor that has stopped returning what this requirement holds, and cannot be lowered to a bound that admits an empty set. Measured before this criterion said any of that, while those values were literals in the test file: five edits confined to that file narrowed what it reaches and left it green at sixteen tests — a criterion struck from its roster, a describe block deleted together with that entry, the pair floors zeroed, the derived pair set filtered down to three, and a row dropped from the enumeration table; the last two admitted one description duplicated across a confusable pair and `update_status` withholding `blocked`, which are the two faults AC-3 and AC-8 were written for. What this does not hold, recorded because a criterion that overclaims is worse than one that concedes: this requirement is not itself anchored, so lowering a number above or striking a criterion still narrows the suite, and it does so in a requirement diff rather than in a test file; the stopword floor refuses an emptied list and not a substituted one; and a floor can be given a second, unreached call site that satisfies the reading check while the assertion that bounded something is gone.
- [x] AC-8: Where a description presents a closed list of argument values, that list equals the runtime constant the tool's own guard decides by, compared in both directions so that a value the description invents and a value it withholds each fail: `update_status` against `REQUIREMENT_STATUSES`, `update_stability` against `STABILITY_LEVELS`, `update_step_state` against the step-state status constant, `set_sds_status` against the SDS status order, and `append_section_note` against the narrative-section allowlist. The comparison needs the list to be findable, so its presentation is part of the contract — one em-dash-delimited run, comma- or `or`-separated — and each code-side constant is asserted non-empty, because an emptied one would make every description agree with it. Measured before this criterion existed: `update_status` withheld `blocked`, `update_step_state` offered `claimed` and `synthesized`, which its guard refuses, `set_sds_status` named no values at all, and `append_section_note` named its three section headings where the argument takes their keys; every other criterion here passed over all four faults.
- [x] AC-9: The suite states the boundary it cannot cross, and demonstrates it rather than narrating it. A `Writes ` clause naming a path the tool never touches satisfies AC-4's rule, and two tools that answer neighbouring questions under unrelated names — `check_vibe_gate` with `workflow_doctor`, `get_next_work_order` with `workflow_next_plan_task` — are never paired by AC-3, so neither of them is ever asked to distinguish itself. Outside AC-8's five enumerations nothing in this file decides whether a description is true of its tool: that judgment needs the handler, the core function and the input schema behind it, which is a source read rather than a string comparison, and it is delegated to an independent reader. If either probe begins to fail, a rule has widened and this criterion and AC-3's floors both need re-reading.

Cited by DESCRIBE BLOCK, not by line. REPORTING, same date: the server build left beforeAll's throw path, because a hook that throws leaves vitest reporting every test in the file as skipped; the two registration mutations that used to report 16 skipped now report 9 failed of 19 and 5 failed of 19. MUTATION TESTING, earlier passes, all red: duplicate description onto a named pair (AC-3), Read-only. marker stripped (AC-4), description shortened under 60 (AC-1), description dropped from the registration payload (AC-2 x2), title re-added (AC-2), judge rewritten into a self-comparison (AC-3+AC-6), judge always clean (AC-6), a describe block deleted (AC-7), the pair rule narrowed to one clause (AC-3+AC-7), the builder widened back to a bare mcpName (five failures), append_section_note's key list replaced by its section headings and one invented key added to it (AC-8 x2). The title mutation SURVIVED the first pass and its assertion was added afterwards; that is why AC-2 names title. LIMITATION, held by AC-9 and by AC-7's closing sentences rather than left in prose: outside AC-8's five enumerations nothing in this file judges whether a description is TRUE of its tool, the confusable-pair rule is a NAME rule so tools with unrelated names that answer nearby questions are never paired, and this requirement is not itself anchored, so lowering a number in AC-7 or striking a criterion still narrows the suite. |

#### Research / Analysis

- -

#### Implementation Notes

- The description belongs on the existing `toolSpecs` registry (FR-ARCH-006 / REL-ARCH-002), not in a table beside it: a name-keyed side table outlives the tool it describes. For the 27 `orchestrate_*` rows the registry is itself derived from `ORCHESTRATE_TOOL_BINDINGS`, so the description is declared on the binding row, which is where that tool is named. Measured before the change: `renderToolNames().length` is 100 (73 literal registry rows plus 27 orchestrate bindings), `toolSpecs.length` is 149, `isReadOnlyTool` accepts 48 of the 100 and `spec.kind === "read"` disagrees with it on none.
- [2026-08-28] Boilerplate disposition, one line per row of audit 28 §4.4's table. Plan §5 asks for these in Change Notes; no MCP or CLI mutation writes a Change Note row without a lifecycle transition, so they are recorded here. `title` — OMITTED: `registerTool`'s config declares `title?: string`, and the SDK's own `getDisplayName` (shared/metadataUtils.js) implements the specification rule that `name` is used for display when `title` is absent, so dropping it costs no display. Held by AC-2.
- [2026-08-28] `"$schema"` — KEPT, and not omittable from this repository. The SDK's `toJsonSchemaCompat` passes only `strictUnions` and `pipeStrategy` to `zodToJsonSchema`, so its `target` defaults to `jsonSchema7`, and `zodToJsonSchema.js` sets `$schema` unconditionally for that target. No option reaches it from here.
- [2026-08-28] `"execution"` — KEPT, and not omittable either: `McpServer.registerTool` passes a hard-coded `{ taskSupport: "forbidden" }` into `_createRegisteredTool`, and its config object has no `execution` key, so the value is not this repository's to send or withhold. `workspaceRoot`'s repeated argument description — UNTOUCHED, as plan §4-7 requires: the repetition is 52 references to one `WORKSPACE_ROOT_SCHEMA` constant, not 52 copies of a string.
- [2026-08-28] Measured payload change, both sides taken from one live `tools/list` over an in-memory transport: 68,064 bytes now, against 52,536 for the same listing rebuilt as it was before — a rise of 15,528 bytes, +29.6%. That reconstruction lands within 10 bytes of the 52,526 audit 28 §4.4 measured on the shipped server, which is what makes the delta comparable. The rise is the trade plan §6 states: 100 descriptions total 17,007 characters, and the dropped title returns about 3,200.
- [2026-08-28] CORRECTION to the measured payload line above, re-taken after the accuracy sweep changed seven descriptions: 68,359 bytes now against the same 52,536 reconstruction, a rise of 15,823 bytes (+30.1%). The 100 descriptions total 17,302 characters, shortest 87, median 172. The earlier figures (68,064 / +15,528 / 17,007) were correct for the pre-sweep text and are superseded by these.
- [2026-08-28] Accuracy sweep, the axis no assertion here covers: seven of the first hundred descriptions were false against source and were corrected. `workflow_latest_artifact` said newest where the resolver sorts by match score first. `check_acceptance_criteria` offered all-at-once, which only the CLI has. `edit_requirement_table_rows` named Change Notes, which it refuses. `init_project` claimed MCP registration and skill installation, which the MCP path never sets.
- [2026-08-28] The other three of that seven: `promote_step_requirement` claimed to remove the step block, which it leaves in place. `add_completed_work` implied a refusal where the core only raises SRS-W015. `workflow_session_status` named one task where the whole pm-state, its stats and its task list return. All seven passed every assertion in this file both before and after correction, which is the shape of the gap AC-3's own note records: two descriptions can differ from each other and both be wrong.
- [2026-08-28] CORRECTION to the sweep line above: `add_completed_work` was corrected in the wrong direction. `addCompletedWork` returns MUTATION_DENIED as soon as `validateCompletedWorkInput` yields any diagnostic, so an unfinished requirement is a refusal and not a warning, and an unregistered target or scope and an unknown id refuse too; `allowIncomplete` waives only the last check. The self-check read the diagnostic severity and not its caller.
- [2026-08-28] Second accuracy sweep, this one by two independent readers working from source rather than by self-check: one took the 73 registry rows, the other the 27 orchestrate bindings. 63 of the 100 descriptions were rewritten — 43 in `src/mcp/schemas.ts` and 20 on the bindings — over roughly seventy findings. Every one of those descriptions had passed every assertion in the suite before and after the first sweep, which is the measurement AC-9 now records instead of leaving to be rediscovered.
- [2026-08-28] The four that inverted a safety property, worst first. `orchestrate_journal_append` said the payload is not schema-checked at append time; `appendWavesLine` validates the journal as it would stand and refuses on any error diagnostic, and its own comment says no flag skips it. `init_project` promised to leave existing files alone, while `force` overwrites the index and scope documents. `orchestrate_issue_defer` claimed to move an issue out of its wave; `closeWave` never reads the deferral.
- [2026-08-28] Three findings were rejected after re-reading the cited lines, because the reader had the code wrong. `claim_step` refuses a verified or frozen requirement only as a `supersede` target, never on `touchesReq`. `workflow_task_check`'s `owner` does not block a first call: the MCP handler defaults it to `kiwi-pm`, so the gate bites only a caller that supplies its own name. `orchestrate_replay_apply` appends each attempt after its dispatch, not before it, and records the outcome with it.
- [2026-08-28] Two binding gaps the sweep found are out of this requirement's scope and were registered as FR-MCP-061 rather than fixed here: `orchestrate_coupling_check` cannot receive a re-partition pass count, so its gate never fires over MCP, and `orchestrate_handoff_validate` cannot receive a run id, so the untested-allowance journal record is unreachable. Both descriptions now name the limit. `orchestrate_validate`'s missing engine argument stays FR-NODE-199 AC-1's.
- [2026-08-28] CORRECTION to both payload lines above, re-measured after the second sweep by the same method: 78,005 bytes now against the same 52,536 reconstruction, a rise of 25,469 bytes (+48.5%). The 100 descriptions total 26,832 characters, shortest 87, median 254, longest 619. The 68,359 / +15,823 / 17,302 figures were correct for the pre-sweep text and are superseded. Accuracy cost 9,530 characters: the caveats a false description omitted are the bulk of the growth.
- [2026-08-28] Anchoring. Mutation testing found five edits that narrowed the suite from inside its own source and left it green at sixteen tests, each turning on a literal the suite used to bound itself. Those values — the criteria roster, the floors, the enumeration roster and the named pairs — now live in this requirement and are read back through parseWorkspace, so narrowing one is a requirement diff. AC-7 names ten floors and states, in its own closing sentences, what that still does not hold.

The file failed, so CI stopped, but a reader scanning counts saw no red. The server build now records its failure and each test that needs the listing re-throws it, so an empty description reports 9 failed of 19 and an undeclared tool reports 5 failed of 19, each naming the tool.

Lowering a number in AC-7 or striking a criterion still narrows the suite; it does so in this document, where a reader of the diff sees the number change. The stopword floor refuses an emptied list and not a substituted one. A floor can be given a second, unreached call site that satisfies the reading check while the assertion that bounded something is gone.

Five literals in `src/mcp/schemas.ts` qualify: `update_status` and `update_stability`, which present a whole enum, and the `Status is verified` guard clause in `edit_requirement_fields`, `replace_acceptance_criteria` and `edit_requirement_table_rows`. Removing one, or adding a sixth, fails FR-FLOW-154's test, not this one.

### FR-MCP-061 — An orchestrate binding that omits a gate-deciding CLI option leaves that behaviour unreachable over MCP, and the description says so until it is declared

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 3.1.0 |
| Status | planned |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | plan-3.0-14, audit-28, follow-up-fr-mcp-060 |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/plan/3.0/14.mcp-tool-description.md |

#### Requirement

Where a CLI leaf option decides a gate refusal or a recorded side effect, the `ORCHESTRATE_TOOL_BINDINGS` row for that leaf SHALL declare the option, so the behaviour behind it is reachable over MCP instead of being fixed at the leaf's default; and for as long as an option stays undeclared, the tool's MCP description SHALL name the behaviour the omission puts out of reach, so an agent choosing the tool is not told it can do what that surface cannot.

#### Rationale

Two rows were measured during the FR-MCP-060 description sweep. `orchestrate_coupling_check` refuses with `stage-coupling-unresolved` when a coupling survives a re-partition pass, but `--repartition-pass` is absent from its binding, so the MCP input schema derived from that list cannot carry it, the value stays at the default 0 and the gate can never fire over MCP. `orchestrate_handoff_validate` journals the untested-AC allowance in force when given `--run-id`, which FR-NODE-155 AC-3 requires so a raised cap is auditable rather than known only to the invocation; neither `--run-id` nor `--journal` is declared, so that record is unreachable from MCP and the verb is read-only there by omission rather than by design. Both are the shape the comment above `orchestrate_round_record`'s binding already names: the input schema is derived from the option list, so an omission leaves a field uncallable over MCP while the CLI accepts it, which is how that tool once shipped uncallable.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: `orchestrate_coupling_check` accepts the re-partition pass count, declared on its binding row so the MCP input schema is derived from it rather than restated beside it, and a stage whose lanes are still coupled at pass 1 refuses with `stage-coupling-unresolved` over MCP exactly as it does at the CLI. Measured at creation: no MCP call can raise that count above the default, so the verb reports couplings and never refuses.
- [ ] AC-2: `orchestrate_handoff_validate` accepts the run id and journal path, so a validation carrying a raised untested-AC allowance records that allowance in the run journal over MCP as it does at the CLI, and a refused append is reported rather than swallowed. Measured at creation: neither option is declared, `journalWritten` is false on every MCP call, and the audit record FR-NODE-155 AC-3 requires is unreachable from that surface.
- [ ] AC-3: For as long as either option stays undeclared, that tool's MCP description names the behaviour the omission puts out of reach and why, and the change that declares an option removes the sentence in the same commit, so the shipped text and the shipped binding never come to disagree in the opposite direction. Measured at creation: both descriptions carry that sentence, held by FR-MCP-060 AC-4's disclosure rule only as far as the marker, not as far as the claim.
- [ ] AC-4: This requirement claims neither of the other two binding gaps the same sweep found. `orchestrate_validate`'s missing engine argument belongs to FR-NODE-199 AC-1, which owns it as a journal-parse discriminator rather than as a gate argument. `orchestrate_route_freeze`'s missing card and journal paths lose no behaviour, because both defaults are the paths the run already uses; what was missing there was the disclosure that the card is written at all, and FR-MCP-060 closed that. Naming both here keeps the family from being read as closed when only half of it is.
- [ ] AC-5: No criterion here asserts the general rule that every option a CLI leaf declares appears on that leaf's binding row. Deriving it needs commander's per-leaf option list, which nothing in this repository reads today, and the rule as stated would fail on the two rows the criterion above disclaims. The scope is the two named rows; the general rule is a separate requirement with its own derivation, and saying so keeps this one from being cited as having swept the table.

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

| --- | --- | --- | --- |

Opened while correcting the FR-MCP-060 descriptions, where the two gaps were found; that item's scope was the descriptions, so the descriptions were made to disclose the limits and the bindings were left alone. The binding rows are `ORCHESTRATE_TOOL_BINDINGS` in `src/cli/commands/orchestrate.ts`; the CLI leaves that already accept the options are `coupling check` and `handoff validate` in the same file.
