### FR-MCP-062 — A derived MCP reader delegates to the reader that owns the fact, and the owner returns it in one round trip

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 3.1.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | plan-3.0-13, audit-28 |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/plan/3.0/13.mcp-response-dedup.md, docs/research/28.skills-mcp-end-to-end-audit.md |

#### Requirement

When a fact one MCP reader returns is computed from what another MCP reader already returned, the owning reader SHALL return that fact itself and the derived reader SHALL delegate to the owner rather than recompute it, so a caller obtains the fact in a single round trip and the rule that produces it exists in exactly one place; the derived tool names SHALL remain registered, because `get_next_work_order` names them to the agent as the next tool to call.

#### Rationale

두 짝은 순수한 왕복 비용이다. `workflowResumeHint` 는 `workflowNextPlanTask` 의 응답에 `blocking !== true && nextTask !== null` 하나를 덧붙이고, `workflowPipelineNext` 는 `workflowPipelineStatus` 의 응답에서 `latestEvent.event.next_hint` 를 꺼내 옆에 놓는다. 두 값 다 원본 응답 안의 필드로 호출자가 계산할 수 있으므로, 원본이 함께 돌려주면 한 번의 왕복으로 끝난다. 그러나 도구 이름을 지우면 `actionTool()` 이 `resume-session` 과 `fix-artifact` 에서 실재하지 않는 이름을 다음 행동으로 돌려주게 되고, 그 실패는 도구를 지운 시점이 아니라 그 두 action 이 처음 선택되는 실행에서 드러난다. 따라서 계산만 옮기고 표면은 남긴다.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Over a real MCP client-server round trip (`tools/call` through an SDK client, not a direct adapter call), `workflow_next_plan_task` returns a `resume` boolean in its `value`, and that boolean equals `blocking !== true && nextTask !== null` computed from the same response, for each of three fixtures: one that yields a next task without blocking, one that blocks, and one that yields no next task. Three predicates coincide here and the reason is the selector's branch structure, not the fixture set: `selectNextTask` answers either a task with an empty `blockedBy` or a null task with a non-empty one, and its caller then replaces `nextTask` with null whenever `blocking` holds, so `blocking !== true && nextTask !== null`, plain `nextTask !== null`, and `nextTask !== null && blockedBy.length === 0` agree on every reachable input. Substituting any one for another is behaviour-preserving and no fixture can separate them. The suite asserts the invariant that makes this so over every plan fixture, so the assertion fires on the day those branches split.
- [x] AC-2: Over the same round trip, `workflow_pipeline_status` returns `nextHint` in its `value`; it equals the `next_hint` of the `latestEvent` the same response carries, and is null when that `latestEvent` is null. It is measured across enough workspaces to answer distinct hints, so a value pinned to any one literal is wrong in another, and one of those journals ends in a logical-delete tombstone so the visible answer differs from the raw last line - a reader taking the unprojected entry set answers the tombstone's hint.
- [x] AC-3: `workflow_resume_hint` remains registered and is listed by a `tools/list` round trip. For arguments identical to a `workflow_next_plan_task` call, its `value` has the same key set and the same value at every key, so the alias contributes nothing of its own; it is called with more than one argument set and answers each differently, so an alias that discarded its arguments would be caught.
- [x] AC-4: `workflow_pipeline_next` remains registered and is listed by a `tools/list` round trip. For identical arguments its `value` has the same key set and the same value at every key as `workflow_pipeline_status`, so an alias that recomputed the hint from the event rather than delegating would be caught by the keys it failed to carry. It is called with a non-default argument that changes the answer on the same workspace, so an alias that discarded its arguments would be caught by the answer it gave instead.
- [x] AC-5: The derived rule exists once. Removing the `resume` computation from `workflow_next_plan_task`, or the `nextHint` computation from `workflow_pipeline_status`, changes what the corresponding alias answers - proven by mutation rather than by reading the source - so no second copy remains that a later edit could leave disagreeing. Alongside that, each alias body is read and required to be a single delegating call, which establishes that no second copy is written and, being a reading of source, establishes nothing about what the shipped tool does.
- [x] AC-6: Every tool name `actionTool()` can return is a member of the tool-name set a `tools/list` round trip reports. The denominator is the whole `WorkOrderAction` union taken from the type rather than a list of the actions this criterion cares about; an action the check cannot resolve to a name fails instead of being skipped. Removing a name from the registry turns the check red, and so does pointing an action at a registered tool no other action uses, because the recorded count of distinct names moves; pointing one at a name another action already uses does not.
- [x] AC-7: The owner/alias relationship is declared in the ToolSpec registry as data, and three things about each of the four descriptions are decided mechanically rather than by reading their prose. First, the description contains verbatim the sentence its own declaration composes. Second, the other tools it names - extracted against a closed set, by MCP spelling for all of them and, for the four that carry a declaration, by CLI spelling and core-function spelling as well - equal what the declaration admits: an owner names no other tool, an alias names only its owner and the tool that keeps it registered. Those two extra spellings are read for those four tools and no further, so a description that names one of the other ninety-six by its CLI or core-function spelling is not read as naming it. Third, the field a declaration names is a key the owner's reply actually carries over the same round trip, so a declaration and a composed sentence that are consistently false about it fail. What is NOT decided is whether the surrounding prose agrees with any of that: a description that refers to the neighbouring tool without naming it in any of those spellings passes, and so does one that carries the composed sentence and contradicts it in the next sentence. Both were measured, and both are enumerated in this requirement's evidence.
- [x] AC-8: The suite runs under numbers this requirement records rather than its own, and it compares them exactly rather than as lower bounds, so lowering one here fails as loudly as raising it: `actions 8`, `tools 100`, `actionTools 6`, `resumeCases 3`, `planFixtures 14`, `hintOutcomes 4`, `derivations 4`. Every one is asserted somewhere in the suite, a number named here that is read nowhere there fails, and the suite fails rather than passing vacuously when this requirement cannot be read at all. `tools` counts what the handler map registers and `tools/list` reports, not what the ToolSpec registry declares: a spec added to that registry without a handler leaves this number where it is, and `FR-MCP-060` AC-5 is what catches that instead, because it compares the names a `tools/list` round trip reports against the names the registry declares. Which criterion fires was planted and measured rather than reasoned about: AC-5 failed on the two name sets while AC-2 stayed green, since `tools/list` still answered a hundred descriptions and every one was still byte-identical to the registry's. AC-4 fails alongside it because its read-only and mutation counts move, but that is a census shifting rather than the registration being compared with the registry.

Two mutation shapes are green by prediction rather than by oversight and each is enumerated in VE-4; both were replanted in round five and came back green. One earlier restatement had found a real defect - an alias-body check that compared the body to a literal string went red on return await origin(root, options) - and one candidate rule was rejected in round four for the same reason before it was written: requiring the composed sentence to be last would have gone red on the appended-clause restatement, so it was not taken and the shape it would have closed is recorded in VE-4 instead. Round five replanted two of those restatements against the widened alphabet - rewording the prose around a composed sentence, and appending an ordinary clause after one - and both stayed green, so the third spelling refuses nothing a correct restatement writes. |

Round six changed prose and comments only - no assertion, no source file - so it closes on the suites that read this document rather than on a second full run, logged at scratchpad/13-r6-closing-suites.log and likewise started after the last byte of this document is written. The lock test test/core/mutation/srs-lock-status-cache.test.ts did not fire in any of these runs and is not reported as green: plan 33 records it as flaky at HEAD. Machine gates on the same bytes: npm run lint exit 0, npm run typecheck:test exit 0, NODE_ENV=development npm run build exit 0, speckiwi validate --json 0 errors and 3 SRS-W015 warnings which is the baseline, speckiwi skills mirror --check --json divergences empty. |
| VE-4 | analysis | measured escapes on AC-7 - descriptions that revive the round trip or name a tool unseen, each planted and observed green | AC-7 | What AC-7 does not catch, enumerated because each one was written into the registry and run rather than reasoned about. (1) An owner description that sends the reader to the neighbouring tool without naming it in any of those spellings - "if all you want is the resume flag, the thin alias beside this tool returns exactly that, so call it instead" - passes, because the negative half extracts names from a closed set and there is no name to extract. (2) An owner or alias description that carries its composed sentence verbatim and then contradicts it in the next sentence - "that sentence is out of date: a second call is in fact still needed" - passes, because the positive half is containment rather than equality, and a rule requiring the composed sentence to be last would have gone red on a correct restatement that appends an ordinary clause, which was measured in the previous round. A third shape, an owner naming the alias by a spelling the protocol itself does not use, was open in both its forms and both are now closed: the extraction reads MCP names for all hundred tools and, for the four that carry a declaration, CLI names and core-function names as well. That reaches those four tools and no further, which leaves a fifth shape open and measured rather than assumed: an owner description that names one of the other ninety-six tools by its CLI or its core-function spelling passes, and so does one that invents a hyphenated spelling the registry binds to nothing. Reading the remaining 192 non-MCP spellings would close the first of those, and it was measured and rejected rather than overlooked - 56 of the CLI names are bare lowercase words such as next, plan, status, resume, pipeline and latest, and reading them raises 13 false positives spread across all four of these descriptions exactly as they ship today. Each widening that was taken was measured for over-correction before it was taken - across all hundred shipped descriptions exactly two contain one of those four CLI names, and the only one on a declared tool is the alias naming its own owner, which its declaration already admits, while none contains one of the four core-function names, so that spelling refuses nothing shipped today. A fourth, a declaration and its composed sentence made consistently false about the field, is also now closed by asserting the declared field is a key the owner's reply carries over the same round trip. This list is not claimed to be exhaustive - it is the set five rounds of adversarial editing produced, and no further vocabulary rule was added because each one closes a phrasing and leaves the next open while making the criterion claim more than it can decide. |

### FR-MCP-063 — A path argument a tool does not accept is refused at the protocol surface

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 3.1.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | 3.1.0, mcp, workspace, root, safety, protocol |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/plan/3.0/31.mcp-workspace-root-silently-dropped.md |

#### Requirement

The MCP SDK registration seam SHALL carry a caller's `root` and `workspaceRoot` arguments to the registration gate instead of dropping them while parsing the tool's declared input schema, so that a tool the gate does not accept a per-call workspace root for answers a `tools/call` request with the gate's refusal rather than with an answer from the startup root. Every tool the gate accepts a `workspaceRoot` for SHALL advertise it in its `tools/list` input schema, the tools that advertise it while the gate refuses it SHALL be exactly the one this requirement names, and the seam SHALL hand a registered tool no key beyond those two that the tool's own shape does not declare as its own property.

#### Rationale

[2026-09-04] `src/mcp/server.ts` hands the SDK the tool's zod raw shape, and a zod object strips keys the shape does not declare, so `"workspaceRoot" in input` in the adapter gate is never true for a tool that does not declare the key. Measured over a real client-server roundtrip on HEAD 1340baca: `list_requirements`, `get_active_target` and `mcp_workspace_info` all answered `server-cwd-discovery` for both `workspaceRoot` and `root`, while the same calls through the adapter handle were refused with MCP_WORKSPACE_ROOT_UNSUPPORTED. `update_status` therefore points at a worktree and writes at the host without saying so. The gate is correct; it is never reached.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Driven over a real MCP client-to-server `tools/call` roundtrip rather than through the in-process adapter handle, every tool the gate refuses a `workspaceRoot` for answers with error code MCP_WORKSPACE_ROOT_UNSUPPORTED and none answers from the startup root. The denominator is the refusing set, read by probing the running server's own registrations through the adapter handle with a workspace root that does not exist so that no handler runs. Its size is not recorded here: which tools refuse is membership, which REL-MCP-005 and the family requirements govern rather than this one, and a figure recorded here goes stale on a change this requirement does not make - it moved from 49 to 36 when FR-MCP-064 opened the SRS query family. The set is instead required to be non-empty and to contain add_requirement and update_status by name, and entries into the call helper are counted and compared for equality with it, because an empty escape list is what a loop that never ran also produces and a collapsed derivation is what two named members cannot be. That counter witnesses entry rather than a completed exchange: the helper it sits in does make the call and reports failures, so it is honest from inside, and a caller that replaced the call site with an increment and a fabricated answer is not caught by it. What a recorded size also caught and this does not - a tool quietly gaining the argument - is held by FR-MCP-064 AC-1, which compares the opened family with the names its own requirement records, in both directions.
- [x] AC-2: The same roundtrip refuses a `root` argument on every registered tool, the tools that do accept `workspaceRoot` included, because `root` is a key no tool accepts. The denominator is the tool list `tools/list` returns, compared for equality with 100, and entries into the call helper are counted and compared for equality with it.
- [x] AC-3: The arguments the seam hands a registered tool are what that tool's own shape declares, plus `root` and `workspaceRoot` when the caller sent them, and nothing else. Membership is decided with hasOwnProperty rather than with `in`: a shape is an ordinary object literal, so `in` answers true for the eleven data properties Object.prototype carries and all eleven would then reach the tool, which is a widening the SDK's stripping object did not have. Three families of undeclared key are driven, each name on its own and all together: two ordinary ones, the inherited eleven, and twelve that sit beside the gate's two - rootPath, rootOverride, roots, workspaceDir, workspaceRootPath, workspaces, myRoot, hostWorkspaceRoot, theRootKey, ROOT, WorkspaceRoot and workspaceroot. Those twelve are sent with three shapes of value, two path-like on either slash and one opaque, so a predicate that reads the value rather than the key is caught as well as one that reads the key; both axes have a case that reddens if the list collapses to one shape. A gate key and an unknown key are also sent in the same call and the result is compared as an exact set, so carrying the one the seam must carry does not admit the one it must drop.
- [x] AC-4: Every tool the gate accepts a `workspaceRoot` for advertises it in the `tools/list` input schema, so no honoured root is hidden from a caller reading the schema. The opposite direction is where the one known disagreement lives and it is held to a name rather than to a count: the set of tools that advertise the argument while the gate refuses it equals exactly `preview_legacy_workflow_migration`, so a second such tool reddens, and so does this one being repaired without the requirement being updated with it. Neither repair is taken here and both are recorded. Dropping the declaration removes one declared argument from the tree; when this was written the tree's total sat exactly on the floor FR-FLOW-162 AC-1 records in its own verified text, so that repair would have broken a verified requirement, and FR-MCP-064 has since lifted the total clear of that floor, which removes the obstacle rather than the decision. Giving the tool the argument for real would add it to the set of tools that accept a per-call root, whose membership REL-MCP-005 governs by naming the scopes a tool may declare, and would leave open which family requirement then owns its behaviour. FR-MCP-058, FR-MCP-059 and FR-MCP-064 do not decide it, because their subjects are the `workflow_*` family, the `orchestrate_*` family and the SRS query family, and this tool belongs to none of the three.
- [x] AC-5: The mechanism is asserted rather than assumed. For a tool that declares neither key, parsing `{root, workspaceRoot}` with `z.object` over that tool's own declared shape yields an object holding neither key, while parsing the same arguments with the schema the server hands the SDK yields both. That difference is why a test calling the adapter handle directly cannot observe what this requirement holds.
- [x] AC-6: The refusal a protocol caller receives is the gate's own structured refusal, carrying the SRS-E075 diagnostic, the reason, the recovery sentence and the workspace identity, rather than a schema validation error, so the caller is told which root answered and what to do instead.
- [x] AC-7: What the suite drives and does not hold is recorded rather than claimed. `__proto__` is sent as a real own key built by JSON.parse, the way the stdio transport builds one, and neither reaches the tool nor writes through to Object.prototype - but measured on zod 4.4.1 the object parse deletes that own key before the narrow runs, so no mutation of the narrow turns that case red and the guard is over the composed pipeline rather than over this requirement's code. The list of inherited names is compared against the computed set, which stops the literal being trimmed but does not stop the computed side being replaced by the literal; the driving list is a literal and complete on this Node, so what is lost is future-proofing rather than coverage. The tables that hold the near-miss list to its loosenings and its value shapes protect the list, not themselves: deleting a row of either is not caught, the same shape as deleting any assertion. And the near-miss names are twelve measured examples, not a census: a predicate that admits some name outside them, or reads something other than the key and the value, is not caught here.

### FR-MCP-064 — Per-call workspaceRoot on the SRS query tools

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 3.1.0 |
| Status | verified |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | - |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | test, inspection |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/plan/3.0/32.srs-read-tools-workspace-root.md |

#### Requirement

SpecKiwi MCP SHALL accept an optional per-call absolute workspaceRoot on the thirteen SRS query tools this requirement names, resolving every read of that call from the named checkout rather than from the startup root, and SHALL leave the argument refused on every other SRS-facing tool, including one that only computes a next free Requirement ID without writing and one whose own answer is which root replied.

#### Rationale

Fifty-one tools take a per-call workspaceRoot and the SRS readers do not, so a caller working in a linked worktree cannot ask what that checkout's SRS says through the surface CLAUDE.md tells agents to prefer. The CLI does answer, taking --root with no validation at all and never reporting which root replied, so today the safe surface is closed and the loose one is open. Opening is confined to reading: a write would allocate Requirement IDs from a per-root counter whose file is gitignored, and the SRS mutation lock is per-root too, so two roots would issue colliding ids while excluding neither.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: The tools that accept workspaceRoot and belong to neither the workflow_* nor the orchestrate_* family are exactly the thirteen this requirement names: list_requirements, search_requirements, get_requirement, validate_spec, summarize_target, get_active_target, list_completed_work, validate_step, get_work_mode, check_vibe_gate, list_dirty_edges, list_compat_edges and list_steps. The set is read from the running server's own registrations rather than from a list written into the test, and compared for equality in both directions, so a fourteenth tool that quietly gains the argument reddens and so does a name silently dropped. Held to names rather than to a count, for the reason FR-MCP-063 AC-4 records: a figure goes stale on a change this requirement does not make.
- [x] AC-2: A call that omits workspaceRoot answers exactly as it does today, with the envelope reporting server-cwd-discovery and the startup root and the answer coming from that root. Asserted on each of the thirteen rather than on one representative, because they split across two different root helpers and threading one helper leaves the other unchanged.
- [x] AC-3: A call naming a linked worktree answers from that worktree. Proven with two checkouts whose docs/spec differ in the value being read, because an implementation that admits the argument at the gate and never threads it to the handler was measured to return the startup root's answer inside an envelope reporting per-call-workspace-root, which satisfies any assertion that only reads the envelope or only checks that the call was not refused. The assertion compares the answer's own content, and compares the envelope's workspaceRoot and rootSource with it.
- [x] AC-4: validate_step resolves both of the roots it reads from the supplied workspaceRoot rather than one of them. It is the only one of the thirteen that calls two different root helpers, so threading one would leave a single call reading two different checkouts. Asserted by giving it a worktree whose step records and whose SRS index both differ from the startup root's.
- [x] AC-5: mcp_workspace_info gains no workspaceRoot, and a test asserts its schema declares neither that key nor root: a tool whose answer is which root replied cannot take that root as an argument without making the comparison FR-FLOW-042 requires read one source twice, which is the ground on which FR-MCP-059 AC-4 excludes orchestrate_preflight. preview_legacy_workflow_migration and get_next_work_order stay closed because their subject is run state under kiwi/ rather than the SRS reading this requirement opens. Which requirement then owns them is deliberately left unsettled here: FR-MCP-063 AC-4 records that preview_legacy_workflow_migration belongs to none of the workflow_*, orchestrate_* and SRS query families, and FR-MCP-058 draws its own family by the workflow_* name prefix that neither tool carries, so naming a governing family here would contradict both. get_next_work_order is named rather than passed over because it does read the SRS - it parses the checkout and returns the active target, that target's requirement ids and the SRS diagnostics the parse produced - and it stays closed all the same, because its subject is the work order it assembles rather than a query a caller aims at a checkout's SRS. The two requirement-id repair readers stay closed because both allocate a Requirement ID, which REL-MCP-005 refuses in its SHALL whether or not the allocation is written.
- [x] AC-6: A workspaceRoot that passes every identity gate but holds no docs/spec/00.index.md is refused before the handler runs, with its own reason and a recovery naming the checkout that was examined rather than a repair command, so a caller who mistyped a sibling worktree is told which root was checked instead of receiving an uncaught ENOENT. Nothing is created by the refusal. The check is asserted to be scoped to the family this requirement opens: a workflow_* tool still answers for a root holding no docs/spec at all, which it does today and which a gate-wide check would break.
- [x] AC-7: Each of the thirteen declares that it takes no caller-supplied path argument, and the set of tools carrying that declaration equals the set of AC-1 in both directions so the two cannot drift apart. That declaration is what lets an SRS query filter on a docs/spec reference, since relatedDoc, evidenceReference and traceReference are filters designed to carry exactly such a path, while REL-MCP-005 AC-6 keeps scanning every argument of a tool that declares nothing.

The three that passed are the ones that describe today's behaviour: the no-argument answers, mcp_workspace_info staying closed, and workflow_doctor answering for a checkout with no docs. Numbers were registered before any edit so that a red and an accidental break could be told apart: the refusing set measured 49 (SRS query 18 + SRS mutation 29 + orchestrate_preflight + orchestrate_replay_apply), accepting 51, advertised 52, and each of the thirteen was inside the refusing set; after the change the same probe reads 36 / 64 / 65, which is the arithmetic and nothing else moving. The advertised-minus-accepting set is still exactly preview_legacy_workflow_migration. AC-3 is measured on the answer's own content, not on the absence of a refusal: two checkouts are built whose docs/spec differ, and each of the thirteen has a value that reads differently in each - the ids list, a marker-bearing search hit, a requirement that is NOT_FOUND at the host, the diagnostic code set, the active target, the completed-work summary, the work mode, the vibe-gate mode, the compat edges and the step list. Load-bearing proved by mutation, run per-file with a harness that scores `no tests`, a collection failure and a zero total as INVALID and fails on an anchor that does not match its declared count. Seventeen inversions, each red: one workspace() call losing the context (1 failed), all eight losing it (2 failed), get_work_mode's projectRoot losing it, list_steps' losing it, all twenty-three losing it, validate_step losing only its step-file root (SDS-E054 instead of SDS-W051), validate_step losing only its workspace root (no SDS diagnostic at all), the opened set losing a name with that tool closed at its registration too (4 failed), the set gaining mcp_workspace_info, the index check removed, the index check made gate-wide (workflow_doctor stops answering), hasSrsIndex forced true, the recovery naming the startup root instead of the checkout, the recovery naming `speckiwi init --force`, callerPathKeys ignored, the declared-key filter inverted, and the rejected readOnlyHint exemption, which reddens this file and takes FR-MCP-058's suite with it. The mutations that came back other than a plain red are recorded rather than argued away, and each is held to its own name rather than to a count, for the reason FR-MCP-063 AC-4 records: a figure goes stale the moment a later round adds one to the list. Dropping a name from the opened set scores INVALID - the run produced no summary line at all - and the honest form of it is the 4-failed case listed above, the same name dropped with that tool closed at its registration too. Giving the opened set a name no schema declares scores INVALID rather than red when the name is planted in the source: withPerCallWorkspaceRoot throws at construction with `declares a per-call workspace root but has no input schema`, so beforeAll never finishes and the guard is fail-loud by design. That fail-loud shape is why this row once left both construction-time guards resting on inspection, and both now carry an assertion instead. `refuses to register a name the thirteen do not declare, and names it` drops one entry from the metadata derivation as it is built - the condition a mistyped registration name produces, and the only one srsReadMetadata can see - then calls registerReadTools on a probe handle and requires the refusal to carry the dropped name; the interception is asserted before the throw is, so a derivation that moves fails the case loudly rather than vacating it. `refuses to derive a schema for an opened name that declares none, and names it` mocks perCallWorkspaceRootTools so server.ts is handed a misspelt name no schema literal declares, and requires the module's own evaluation to reject naming it. Neither pins the message text, so rewording one stays free; each requires the name, because a guard that refuses without saying which name it refused leaves a startup failure with no way to find the typo. Both were then proved load-bearing by removal in an isolated copy, one failing case each and each restored under md5: srsReadMetadata's throw deleted, replaced by the silent metadata fallback its own comment names, and rewritten to throw without the name; withPerCallWorkspaceRoot's throw deleted, replaced by a skip over the misspelt tool, and rewritten to throw without the name. What this does not repair: the per-run pass figures recorded in this row were measured before those two cases existed, so each is one round behind and is left as measured rather than rewritten - a figure edited without the run behind it is worse than a stale one. VE-2 was re-measured instead, by a closing run made after those cases landed. Loosening the gate's closed-set membership test to a presence test is likewise green here at 10 passed, because every registration on this tree carries one of the two spellings and only a synthetic misspelt tool separates them; the same mutation measured against REL-MCP-005's own suite is red at 1 failed / 14 passed, which is where that case lives. Ten behaviour-preserving restatements, all green: some() for includes, Boolean() for a === true, indexOf for includes, Object.assign for a spread, the opened set reordered, entries() for keys().filter(), a reversed equality, the nullish root choice written out as an if/else, an undefined compare for a falsy one, and the schema derivation passed through a copy - that last one is residue: the keys are asserted, the fact that they come from one declaration is not, and a literal drifting from the gate is caught by FR-MCP-063 AC-4 rather than here. |

| Requirement | REL-MCP-005 | depends_on | The gate that decides which root is acceptable and who may name one. Its SHALL had to widen before this family could declare a scope at all, and its AC-6 keeps owning the destination rule this requirement's tools opt out of by declaring no caller-supplied path. |

#### Change Notes

| Date | Change | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-04 | Requirement created | 5인 결정위원회가 계획 32번의 결정 다섯을 정하고 이 요구의 경계를 그었다 — 개방 열셋, repair 진단·계획 제외, 경로 키 선언 방식, 인덱스 부재의 구조화된 거절, 요구 셋 구조. 근거는 docs/plan/3.0/32.srs-read-tools-workspace-root.md 9절. |
| 2026-09-05 | Status -> verified | AC 일곱 전부 증거와 함께 확인. 독립 서브에이전트 네 축(뮤테이션·AC 대조·사실성·배선)이 각각 재었고 구현 결함 0건. 배선 축이 열셋 전부를 세 서버 왕복(호스트 무인자 · 워크트리 네이티브 · 호스트에 인자)으로 재어 답의 본문이 워크트리 것임을 확인했고, 뮤테이션 축이 41건 중 GREEN 4건은 전부 동작-보존 재서술임을 보였다. 사실성 축이 두 요구가 서로를 부정하는 자리와 VE 의 수치 오기를 잡아 수정했다. 마감 회귀 469 파일 7794 통과 0 실패, 로그 mtime 이 모든 변경 파일보다 나중이다. |
